# Changelog

All notable changes to this repository are documented in this file.

The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), with one repo-specific section added: **Verified Tool Snapshots**.

---

## Versioning policy

This repository is versioned semantically, with content-aware semantics:

- **Major version (X.0.0):** A reorganization that breaks existing links or the learning-path structure. Rare.
- **Minor version (0.X.0):** New content tracks, new learning paths, or substantial expansions to existing sections.
- **Patch version (0.0.X):** Individual concept, lab, recipe, or pattern additions; tool-snapshot updates; bug fixes; typo fixes.

Releases are tagged on `main` as `vX.Y.Z`. The `CHANGELOG.md` is the source of truth for what changed; GitHub release notes link back to it.

Because this is an evolving educational resource and not a software library, we don't promise backwards-compatible URLs across major versions. We do promise to provide migration notes when major reorganizations happen.

---

## Tool snapshot policy

Pages in `tools/` and any code that depends on a specific framework version carry a snapshot date in this form:

```
> 🔴 Tool snapshot — <tool> v<version>, verified YYYY-MM-DD
> Source: <official docs / changelog / spec link>
```

Tool snapshots are tracked separately in the **Verified Tool Snapshots** section of each release. When you update a snapshot:

1. Update the badge on the relevant `tools/<tool>/` page.
2. Add a line to the current `[Unreleased]` section under **Verified Tool Snapshots**.
3. Include a primary-source link in your PR description.

Routine snapshot sweeps happen at minor releases. Individual updates land continuously between releases.

When a tool ships a breaking change (e.g., `langgraph.prebuilt` → `langchain.agents`), the snapshot update is paired with a migration note on the tool page and, if needed, code updates across labs and recipes.

---

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **🧪 OCR-reading on real images, and making the staged pieces real (Batch 85).** Module 29 reads real pixels with a real OCR engine and turns the prior modules' guarded wirings into ones verified against the real wire format. One new lab on real OCR, a new math-foundations page on edit-distance alignment, a concept note with a pipeline diagram, and four real-technique/real-schema upgrades. The through-line: OCR failures are emergent - you render real images, run a real engine, and grade legibility and value separately - and the same discipline (parse the real format, reproduce CI locally) turns "guarded" into "verified" before live infrastructure is involved.

  - **🧪 [Lab 63: OCR-reading on real rendered images](labs/63-ocr-on-real-images/)** renders a deterministic image corpus across a degradation ladder (clean, sensor noise, blur, small-font blur, heavy), runs real tesseract OCR, and grades each read by CER and a value-aware answer check. On this corpus tesseract reads the clean and mildly-blurred images perfectly, returns a blank on heavy noise (a read failure), garbles a small blurred line to "hooey", and misreads "9.4 billion" as "94 bien" - a ~10^8 value error that CER alone (0.27) would wave through. A recorded fixture keeps the grading deterministic without tesseract; `--live` runs the real engine when it is installed.
  - **📐** New [math-foundations/18: Edit-distance alignment and error decomposition](math-foundations/18-edit-distance-alignment.md) - backtracing the Levenshtein DP to an alignment, the decomposition $d = S + I + D$ into substitutions/insertions/deletions, the character confusion profile, and why a single deleted decimal point is one unit of CER and a tenfold value error.
  - **📖** New concept note [ocr-on-real-images.md](concepts/rag/ocr-on-real-images.md) with a pipeline diagram (source → crop → preprocess → OCR → normalize → grade) covering real failure modes mapped to S/I/D error types, preprocessing that helps and hurts, and why you measure on real images rather than assume.
  - **🔧 Real-technique / real-schema upgrades** of the prior suggested-next items: [`compare.py`](labs/59-retuning-on-held-out-pairs/compare.py) adds a model-free dense embedder (the hashing trick) so three real geometries are compared, plus a `SentenceTransformerEmbedder` adapter; [`trace_backend.py`](labs/56-production-traces-routing/trace_backend.py) parses the real Grafana Tempo search schema (OTLP-JSON attributes), verified against a recorded fixture; [`generate.py`](labs/52-red-teaming-trajectories/generate.py) reads the real garak `report.jsonl` format (`entry_type`/`probe_classname` normalized); and [`ops/ci-local/`](ops/ci-local/) reproduces the CI integration jobs locally (offline tier + live tier against Redis + LocalStack).
  - The glossary gains 6 terms (alignment, edit operations, feature hashing, image preprocessing, OCR engine, read failure vs misread); [Path 02](learning-paths/02-agentic-rag/) gains Module 29; the landing page lab count is updated to 63 and math to 18.

  Verified deterministically: `images.py --self-test` (5 images rendered byte-identically across builds), `read.py --self-test` (graded the recorded real-OCR fixture - answer accuracy 0.40, mean CER 0.43, attribution {correct 2, read_failure 1, numeric_misread 2}; the 9.4-billion-as-94 misread is a numeric_misread; with tesseract present, `--live` confirms clean=perfect and degradation breaks reads), `compare.py --self-test` (three embedder geometries: optimism +0.031 / +0.044 / +0.053 at n=16, all vanishing), `trace_backend.py --self-test` (JsonlBackend reproduces eval cost; the Tempo parser reads the real search schema from the fixture), `generate.py --self-test` (5 trajectories, 2 from a real garak report.jsonl), and the `ops/ci-local` offline tier runs the same self-tests CI runs. Both new notebooks execute; all YAML/compose parse. Citations are real (tesseract; Levenshtein edit distance; the hashing trick; Grafana Tempo search API + OTLP-JSON; garak report.jsonl; LocalStack; GitHub Actions). Staged behind live dependencies and documented rather than run here: a real vision-language embedder + generator (Lab 63 grades real OCR, but the generator/grounding stage on real images is still future work), a real sentence-transformer download for `compare.py`, a live Tempo query for `TempoBackend`, live garak/PyRIT runs and a keyed `AnthropicJudge`, and the CI workflows and `docker compose` live tier. Lab data is fictional and grounded in the Batch 71 corpus; OCR output is from tesseract 5.3.4 and will drift across versions (hence the recorded fixture); model prices and the tool landscape are fast-moving and flagged as such.

- **🧪 Multimodal evaluation in depth: retrieval, grounding, and OCR-reading (Batch 84).** Module 28 grades multimodal RAG instead of just running it: a multimodal answer can fail at retrieval, OCR-reading, or grounding, and a single accuracy number cannot tell them apart. Two labs that grade the three axes separately and attribute every failure to its cause, a new math-foundations page, a concept note with two diagrams, and four real-wiring add-ons that take the prior suggested-next items the rest of the way. The through-line: grounded is not correct, and one accuracy number hides three bugs.

  - **🧪 [Lab 61: Grading multimodal RAG by stage](labs/61-grading-multimodal-rag/)** computes retrieval recall, OCR CER, and grounding rate as separate metrics, then attributes every wrong answer to its first failing stage (retrieval -> OCR -> grounding). The decisive result: a noisy-OCR run and an ungrounded run both score 0.75 end-to-end, but the attribution shows one is an OCR bug (grounding still 1.00 - the generator faithfully repeated a misread number) and the other a grounding bug (CER clean - a hallucination). Same accuracy, different fix.
  - **🧪 [Lab 62: OCR-reading quality](labs/62-ocr-reading-quality/)** builds CER/WER, normalization, and a value-aware numeric match, and shows the two-sided trap: a misread decimal ("4.2 million" -> "42 million") is only ~0.09 CER but a 10x value error, while a reformat ("$4.2M") is ~0.7 CER but numerically identical. Surface metrics mislead both ways; numeric answers need a value-aware check.
  - **📐** New [math-foundations/17: Multimodal evaluation metrics](math-foundations/17-multimodal-eval-metrics.md) - the Levenshtein recurrence, CER/WER, why CER is wrong for numbers, grounding as conditional accuracy, and the end-to-end decomposition $\Pr[C]=\Pr[R]\Pr[O|R]\Pr[G|O]$ that makes accuracy non-invertible and forces per-stage attribution.
  - **📖** New concept note [grounding-and-ocr.md](concepts/rag/grounding-and-ocr.md) with a pipeline diagram and a failure-attribution decision tree: grounding vs faithfulness vs correctness, and how to build an eval set whose answers live only in the image.
  - **🔧 Real-wiring add-ons** for the prior suggested-next items: [`generate.py`](labs/52-red-teaming-trajectories/generate.py) (batch a directory of garak/PyRIT/AgentDojo outputs into a trajectories JSONL), [`trace_backend.py`](labs/56-production-traces-routing/trace_backend.py) (a `TraceBackend` protocol - JSONL now, guarded Tempo when live), [`smoke_sqs.py`](labs/57-real-backends-integration/smoke_sqs.py) + [`.github/workflows/nightly-smoke.yml`](.github/workflows/nightly-smoke.yml) (a nightly LocalStack SQS liveness check), and [`compare.py`](labs/59-retuning-on-held-out-pairs/compare.py) (compare the optimism curve across embedders).
  - The glossary gains 9 terms (CER, WER, edit distance, error attribution, grounding rate, numeric tolerance, OCR-reading, smoke test, trace backend); [Path 02](learning-paths/02-agentic-rag/) gains Module 28; the landing page lab count is updated to 61 and math to 17.

  Verified deterministically: `mm_eval.py --self-test` (shared-space all-retrieval-failures; clean caption 1.00; noisy-OCR 0.75 with grounding 1.00 attributed to OCR; ungrounded 0.75 with clean CER attributed to grounding - same accuracy, different cause), `ocr_eval.py --self-test` (WER 1/3 for a dropped word; normalization 0.18->0.00 for cosmetics; misread-decimal CER 0.09 but numeric_match False; format-diff CER 0.73 but numeric_match True), `compare.py --self-test` (optimism +0.031 char-trigram vs +0.044 word-bag at n=16, both vanishing), `trace_backend.py --self-test` (JsonlBackend reproduces the eval cost), `generate.py --self-test` (3 tool files -> 3 validated trajectories), `smoke_sqs.py --self-test` (SQS round-trip via moto). Both new solution notebooks execute; the new YAML parses. Citations are real (Levenshtein edit distance; CER/WER as OCR/ASR metrics; CLIP/SigLIP; OpenTelemetry/Tempo; GitHub Actions schedule + service containers; LocalStack; garak/PyRIT/AgentDojo). Staged behind live dependencies and documented rather than run here: a real vision-language embedder + generator (the stand-ins engineer the failures a real model would grade), a real sentence-transformer for `compare.py`, a live Tempo query for `TraceBackend`, live garak/PyRIT runs for `generate.py`, and the CI workflows (GitHub Actions). Lab data is fictional and grounded in the Batch 71 corpus; the "CLIP can't read" effect uses disjoint visual / in-image-text vocabularies; model prices and the tool landscape are fast-moving and flagged as such.

- **🧪 From offline to live: CI, distribution shift, and multimodal (Batch 83).** Module 27 takes the Module 26 work the rest of the way - running the real-client tests against live infrastructure in CI, tuning thresholds out-of-sample when re-fitting on real embeddings, pointing the trace pipeline at a real collector, and making multimodal retrieval runnable. Two new labs, a new math-foundations page, a concept note with CI and trace-pipeline diagrams, and ops artifacts (a CI workflow + an OTel collector bundle). A deliberate spread of file types across the repo. The through-line: a test pyramid for external dependencies, and the discipline that a tuned threshold is a fitted parameter.

  - **🧪 [Lab 59: Re-tuning the threshold on held-out pairs](labs/59-retuning-on-held-out-pairs/)** answers the "re-tune on held-out pairs" suggestion with the statistics behind it. `retune.py` builds overlapping reflow/edit pairs (paraphrases that drop the cosine, negations that barely move it) and measures the *optimism* of in-sample threshold selection by sample size - ~+0.03 at n=16, vanishing by n=160 - then fixes it with cross-validated held-out accuracy. Anchored by the new [math-foundations/16](math-foundations/16-threshold-selection-under-shift.md).
  - **🧪 [Lab 60: Multimodal RAG, runnable](labs/60-multimodal-rag-runnable/)** makes the Batch 82 multimodal concept page executable. `multimodal.py` indexes a corpus two ways - shared-space (CLIP/SigLIP) vs caption-then-embed - and reproduces the "CLIP can't read" failure: visual queries recall@1 1.00 for both, text-in-image queries 0.00 for shared-space vs 1.00 for caption-then-embed, with retrieval and grounding scored separately.
  - **📐** New [math-foundations/16: Threshold selection under distribution shift](math-foundations/16-threshold-selection-under-shift.md) (in-sample optimism via Jensen, the $\sqrt{2\log m / n}$ scaling, held-out / cross-validation, covariate shift). The math-foundations index gains rows for pages 15 and 16.
  - **📖⚙️** New concept note [from-offline-mocks-to-live-ci.md](concepts/observability/from-offline-mocks-to-live-ci.md) with a CI test-pyramid diagram and an OTLP trace-pipeline diagram, covering backend integration in CI (item 1), a real collector (item 3), and red-team generation + judge in CI (item 4). Ops artifacts: [`.github/workflows/backend-integration.yml`](.github/workflows/backend-integration.yml) (runs Lab 57's skip-unless-live test against real Redis + LocalStack service containers) and [`ops/otel-collector/`](ops/otel-collector/) (collector config + docker-compose + wiring guide).
  - The glossary gains 6 terms (cross-validation, in-sample optimism, covariate shift, OTLP collector, CI service container, shared-space embedding); [Path 02](learning-paths/02-agentic-rag/) gains Module 27; the landing page lab count is updated to 59 and the math-foundations count to 16.

  Verified deterministically: `retune.py --self-test` (optimism +0.031 at n=16 shrinking to +0.000 by n=160; in-sample never below held-out), `multimodal.py --self-test` (visual recall@1 1.00 both; text-in-image 0.00 shared vs 1.00 caption; grounding separated from retrieval). Both solution notebooks execute. The three new YAML files (CI workflow, collector config, docker-compose) parse. Citations are real (k-fold cross-validation; Jensen's inequality / maximization bias; covariate shift; OpenTelemetry Collector + OTLP; CLIP / SigLIP; GitHub Actions service containers; LocalStack). What is staged behind live dependencies and documented rather than run here: the CI workflow runs in GitHub Actions (the YAML is provided and parses); a real sentence-transformer re-tune (the procedure is identical, guarded import); the OTel collector + backend (compose + config provided); and a real vision-language embedder for multimodal (the stand-in guarantees the failure a real model would grade). Lab data is fictional and grounded in the Batch 71 corpus; the multimodal "CLIP can't read" effect is engineered by disjoint visual / in-image-text vocabularies; model prices and the tool landscape are fast-moving and flagged as such.

- **🧪 Making it real, and the retrieval science we skipped (Batch 82).** Module 26 takes the last step from Module 25's production *forms* to the actual client libraries and tools, and fills two retrieval-science gaps the path skipped. Two new labs, three real-wiring add-ons to the Module 25 labs, and two concept pages. The through-line: "real" means the actual library, the actual tool, and a test that runs against live infrastructure - and the science you skipped will bite in production.

  - **🧪 [Lab 57: Real durable backends, integration-tested](labs/57-real-backends-integration/)** replaces Lab 54's hand-written fakes with the actual client libraries. `backends.py` issues real `redis-py` Streams commands (`XADD` / `XREADGROUP` / `XACK` / `XAUTOCLAIM` / `XPENDING`) and real `boto3` SQS calls (`SendMessage` / `ReceiveMessage` / `DeleteMessage` with a server-side redrive policy); the self-test exercises that exact code through `fakeredis` and `moto`, which run the real commands in-process, and `test_integration.py` runs it against a live Redis (`REDIS_URL`) or LocalStack (`AWS_ENDPOINT_URL`), skipping when neither is set. Both backends reach the identical end state from two unrelated give-up mechanisms (consumer-group PEL vs SQS `maxReceiveCount`).
  - **🧪 [Lab 58: Measuring lost-in-the-middle](labs/58-measuring-lost-in-the-middle/)** builds the position-sweep methodology for the Liu et al. (2023) finding that long-context accuracy is U-shaped in the gold passage's position. `lostmiddle.py` recovers the curve, shows mean accuracy hides the bias, and quantifies rerank-to-top and shrink-k mitigations. Companion concept page [concepts/rag/lost-in-the-middle.md](concepts/rag/lost-in-the-middle.md).
  - **🔧 Real-wiring add-ons** to the Module 25 labs: [`embedders.py`](labs/55-calibrated-detection-judgment/embedders.py) makes the change-detection embedder a swappable dependency (a guarded real `SentenceTransformerEmbedder`, char-trigram as the offline default, `tune_with` re-runs the ROC/Youden selection with any embedder); [`eval_service.py`](labs/56-production-traces-routing/eval_service.py) serializes the GenAI spans to a trace store and runs the cost loop as a separate reader with no access to the agent's objects; [`redteam_adapters.py`](labs/52-red-teaming-trajectories/redteam_adapters.py) turns garak / PyRIT / AgentDojo output into the Lab 52 schema and backs the `llm_judge` hook with an `AnthropicJudge` (injectable `complete_fn`, testable offline, real model in production, may only tighten an axis).
  - **📖** New retrieval-science page [concepts/rag/multimodal-rag.md](concepts/rag/multimodal-rag.md) (shared-space vs caption-then-embed, mixed-content chunking, reranking, grounding/OCR eval). The [concepts/rag](concepts/rag/) README gains both new pages; the glossary gains 10 terms (fakeredis, moto, LocalStack, integration test, lost-in-the-middle, multimodal RAG, CLIP, SigLIP, caption-then-embed, sentence-transformer); [Path 02](learning-paths/02-agentic-rag/) gains Module 26; the landing page lab count is updated to 57.

  Verified deterministically: `backends.py --self-test` (real redis-py via fakeredis == real boto3 SQS via moto: m0 acked, m1 recovered on redelivery, m2 gave up to the dead set), `pytest test_integration.py` (2 skipped with no servers; same backends run against live infrastructure), `lostmiddle.py --self-test` (k=20 edges ~0.95 vs middle ~0.50; mean over random placement ~0.64; rerank-to-top ~0.95; middle accuracy 0.50 -> 0.77 as k shrinks 40 -> 6), `redteam_adapters.py --self-test` (garak/PyRIT/AgentDojo adapters produce scoreable prompt_injection / goal_hijack / excessive_agency trajectories; AnthropicJudge catches a paraphrased leak via an injected model and fails safe on bad output), `embedders.py --self-test` (swappable embedder reproduces the tuned threshold; real model guarded), and `eval_service.py --self-test` (72 spans serialized; the separate reader computes the same cost across a file boundary). Both solution notebooks per new lab execute end-to-end. Citations are real (redis-py Streams consumer groups; Amazon SQS visibility timeout / redrive; LocalStack; `fakeredis`; `moto`; Liu et al. 2023 lost-in-the-middle, TACL; NVIDIA garak / Microsoft PyRIT / AgentDojo; sentence-transformers / all-MiniLM-L6-v2; OpenTelemetry OTLP; CLIP / SigLIP). The self-tests run the real `redis-py` / `boto3` code via `fakeredis` / `moto` offline; a real sentence-transformer, garak/PyRIT runs, an OTLP collector, and a vision-language embedder still need their live dependencies and are staged behind guarded imports or documented as the next step. Lab data is fictional and grounded in the Batch 71 corpus; the lost-in-the-middle answerer is a deterministic stand-in for a model's position bias; model prices and the tool landscape are fast-moving and flagged as such.

- **🧪 From stand-ins to production — productionizing the observability arc (Batch 81).** Module 25 swaps each observability stand-in built in Modules 16–24 for the form you run in production, keeping the contract the stand-in defined. Three new labs plus an upgrade to Lab 52, anchored by a new math-foundations page and a concept note, and threaded through the glossary, learning path, and landing page. The through-line: every stand-in becomes the thing you run, and the contract is what makes the swap safe.

  - **🧪 [Lab 54: Production durable backends](labs/54-production-durable-backends/)** implements Lab 50's lease/ack/reclaim/give-up contract on two production backends. `backends.py` provides `RedisStreamQueue` (consumer group + Pending Entries List + `XAUTOCLAIM`) and `SQSQueue` (visibility timeout + redrive policy to a DLQ); the Lab 50 redelivery worker drives either unchanged and both produce the identical end state. Real `redis` / `boto3` paths sit behind guarded imports; behavior is verified against in-process fakes that mirror each system's semantics.
  - **🧪 [Lab 55: Calibrated detection and judgment](labs/55-calibrated-detection-judgment/)** replaces two stand-ins. Part A tunes the change-detection cosine threshold on labeled reflow/edit pairs by Youden's J instead of a fixed 0.98. Part B replaces Lab 51's single additive judge shift with isotonic regression (pool-adjacent-violators) and the all-dimensions-pass gate with a weighted gate. Anchored by the new [math-foundations/15](math-foundations/15-calibration-threshold-selection.md).
  - **🧪 [Lab 56: Production traces and routing](labs/56-production-traces-routing/)** runs the eval and cost loops on real OpenTelemetry spans. `traces.py` instruments steps as GenAI-semantic-convention spans, reconstructs them from the exported spans (so the loop runs on the trace store, not the agent's objects), and replaces Lab 53's hand-set `simple` flag with a learned routing classifier that has its own held-out eval.
  - **🧪 [Lab 52](labs/52-red-teaming-trajectories/) upgrade** adds a pluggable `llm_judge` to `redteam_score.py` that catches the paraphrased leak the keyword detector missed (scorer-vs-gold agreement 0.98 → 1.00; the judge may only tighten an axis), plus a section on generating trajectories with garak / PyRIT / AgentDojo.
  - **📐📖** New [math-foundations/15: Calibration and threshold selection](math-foundations/15-calibration-threshold-selection.md) (ROC / Youden's J, isotonic regression / PAVA, conjunctive vs weighted decisions) and concept note [from-stand-ins-to-production.md](concepts/observability/from-stand-ins-to-production.md) with a pipeline diagram. The glossary gains 16 terms (Redis Streams consumer group, Pending Entries List, SQS visibility timeout, dead-letter queue, isotonic regression, PAVA, Youden's J, ROC curve, weighted/conjunctive gate, OpenTelemetry GenAI semantic conventions, span, garak, PyRIT, AgentDojo, LLM-as-judge); the [Path 02](learning-paths/02-agentic-rag/) README gains Module 25; the landing page lab and math-foundations counts are corrected.

  Verified deterministically: `backends.py --self-test` (one contract, two backends; m0 acked, m1 recovered on redelivery, m2 gave up to the dead set — identical on Redis Streams and SQS), `calibrate.py --self-test` (tuned threshold acc 0.96 / FPR 0.08 vs fixed 0.98 acc 0.83 / FPR 0.33; completeness QWK on held-out 0.70 raw → 0.88 additive → 0.92 isotonic; weighted gate differs from all-dims on a faithfulness-strong release), `traces.py --self-test` (72 GenAI spans exported; cost loop on spans matches source; routing classifier precision 1.00 / recall 0.92, learned saving 25% of a 33% oracle — the gap is misroutes), and `redteam_score.py --self-test` (keyword agreement 0.98 → 1.00 with the LLM judge; the rt03 paraphrased leak flips). Labs are self-contained; both solution notebooks per lab execute end-to-end. The real OpenTelemetry SDK is used with an in-memory exporter (offline). Citations are real (Redis Streams consumer-group semantics; Amazon SQS visibility timeout and redrive; OWASP Top 10 for LLM and Agentic Applications; NVIDIA garak / Microsoft PyRIT / AgentDojo; OpenTelemetry GenAI semantic conventions; isotonic regression via pool-adjacent-violators, `sklearn.isotonic` equivalent; Youden's J). Lab data is fictional, grounded in the Batch 71 corpus; the embedder is a deterministic char-trigram stand-in (production: a sentence-transformer) and the backends run on fakes (production: `redis-py` / `boto3`); model prices and the tool landscape are fast-moving and flagged as such.

- **🧪 Completing the observability pillars — red teaming and cost (Batch 80).** Two labs that build the fourth observability pillar and the cost dimension the Batch 79 guide put on the PM, so every pillar now has a hands-on lab. The through-line: the four pillars are one discipline — red teaming is evaluation pointed at an adversary, and cost observability is alerting pointed at the bill.

  - **🧪 [Lab 52: Red-teaming agent trajectories](labs/52-red-teaming-trajectories/)** builds the pillar the guide left conceptual. `redteam_score.py` grades recorded adversarial trajectories on three axes — `tool_selection` (excessive agency, tool misuse), `recovery` (prompt injection, goal hijacking), and `containment` (information leakage) — each mapped to the OWASP Agentic Top 10, then computes per-category pass rates and a release gate. `redteam_trajectories.jsonl` holds 20 trajectories (four categories × 5), grounded in the Batch 71 corpus. A `red-team-gate.yml` workflow runs the scorer in CI and blocks a release when any category falls below the bar.
  - **🧪 [Lab 53: Cost and latency observability](labs/53-cost-latency-observability/)** makes per-session cost measurable. `cost.py` accounts cost across tokens, runtime, and tool calls; surfaces the p90/p99 tail the mean hides; detects runaway loops (a repeated tool-call signature); quantifies the re-sent-context share of the bill; and simulates prompt caching and model routing. `sessions.jsonl` holds 40 synthetic traces (36 normal, 4 runaway loops).
  - **📖** The [observability guide](concepts/observability/observability-for-agent-pms.md) and its section README are updated to point the red-teaming pillar at Lab 52 and the cost metric at Lab 53 — closing the "lab on the roadmap" note from Batch 79.

  Verified deterministically: `redteam_score.py --self-test` (scorer-vs-human-gold agreement 0.98 — reliable but missing one paraphrased leak the keyword detector slips past; weakest category excessive agency at 60%; the gate blocks below an 80% bar; the leakage detector catches the injection exploit) and `cost.py --self-test` (p99 ≈ 10× the mean so the tail dominates; the four runaway loops flagged exactly; re-sent context 61% of normal-session input tokens and ~90% overall; prompt caching ~54% off; routing the routine workload ~47% off). Percentiles, agreement, and cost are computed with no third-party libraries; both labs run offline with no model or network. Citations are real (OWASP Top 10 for LLM and Agentic Applications; NVIDIA garak / Microsoft PyRIT / AgentDojo / AgentHarm for adversarial trajectories; the 2026 cost figures — re-sent context as a majority of agent bills, the heavy-tailed per-session distribution, and model routing as the routine-spend control — are web-sourced and flagged as fast-moving); lab data is fictional, grounded in the Batch 71 corpus; token counts in Lab 53 are illustrative (the ratios are the lesson, not the dollar amounts).

- **🧪 Observability for agent PMs — closing both loops (Batch 79).** A PM-facing concept guide plus two labs that close the operations and evaluation loops Batch 78 left open. The through-line: a failure is the start of a loop, not the end of one — and the PM owns the bar while engineering owns the wiring.

  - **📖 [Observability for AI Agent PMs](concepts/observability/observability-for-agent-pms.md)** (new `concepts/observability/` section) covers why observability became the priority for agent teams in 2026, the four pillars (traces, evals, alerts, red teaming) and how they form one trace → eval → fix loop, and where the PM's job ends and engineering's begins — with the metrics a PM should own (task success, quality vs graded gold, cost per session mean/p90, escalation rate, drift, red-team pass rate). The pillars map to the Path 02 production tail; the red-teaming pillar is covered conceptually with a lab on the roadmap.
  - **🧪 [Lab 50: Closing the failure loop](labs/50-closing-the-failure-loop/)** (ops) turns Lab 48's inspect-only dead-letter queue into a durable one. New `dlq.py` provides a `DurableQueue` (file-locked, `pending`/`leased`/`done`/`dead`, lease/ack/nack, `compact` retention) and a `redeliver` worker that leases each failed page, re-sends it, acks the ones that land, and gives up past `max_attempts`; `notify.py` gains a `--redeliver` worker and a `--durable-queue` flag that is drop-in for `--dead-letter`. `canary.py` adds a semantic fingerprint (`bow_vector` / `cosine` / `semantic_changed`) so a meaning-preserving rewrap — which normalized hashing still flags — no longer triggers review. The nightly workflow points `--durable-queue` at the queue and adds a redelivery step.
  - **🧪 [Lab 51: Calibrated, multi-dimensional judgment](labs/51-calibrated-multidimensional/)** (eval) acts on Lab 49's one-level-low finding: re-derive the judge ceiling on the ordinal scale per dimension (inter-expert Krippendorff α as the cap), calibrate the judge's bias on a held-out split instead of discarding the judge, re-threshold the gate, and grade faithfulness × relevance × completeness at once.

  Each lab ships the house structure (README brief, scaffold `lab.ipynb` with `TODO` cells, `solution/` with notes and reference notebook). Verified deterministically: `dlq.py --self-test` (enqueue → redeliver acks the healthy pages and re-pends the flaky one, a second drain acks it, a permanent failure goes `dead` after max attempts, `compact` drops old `done`); a closed-loop integration where `notify.deliver` dead-letters to a `DurableQueue` and the worker drains it; `canary.py --self-test` (a rewrap differs by normalized hash but `semantic_changed` is `False`, a content edit is flagged); and for Lab 51, the ceiling capping the judge on every dimension (faithfulness 1.00 ≥ 0.92, relevance 1.00 ≥ 0.91, completeness 0.97 ≥ 0.50), calibration lifting held-out completeness QWK 0.50 → 1.00, the re-thresholded gate going from precision 1.00 / recall 0.55 to 1.00 / 0.82 (the uncalibrated completeness bias was falsely blocking good releases), and per-dimension QWK naming completeness as the weak judge. Ordinal α and quadratic-weighted κ are implemented inline (no `statsmodels`); the durable queue is a file with a lock (production uses Redis streams or SQS with the same lease/ack/retention contract); the semantic embedder is a deterministic bag-of-words stand-in (production uses a sentence-transformer and tunes the cosine threshold on held-out pairs). Citations are real (Redis-streams / SQS lease-ack and dead-letter patterns; OWASP Top 10 for LLM and Agentic Applications; OpenTelemetry GenAI semantic conventions; NIST AI RMF; EU AI Act adversarial-testing obligations; Krippendorff 1980 ordinal α; Cohen quadratic-weighted κ); the observability landscape in the guide is web-sourced for 2026 and flagged as fast-moving; lab data is fictional, grounded in the Batch 71 corpus. The Lab 46/48 toolkit scripts are updated in place (additive; supersede the Batch 78 copies).

- **🧪 Real backends and graded judgments (Batch 78).** Two labs that swap Batch 77's stand-ins for the things you actually run and move the evaluation anchor from binary to graded — one on each side — plus a small repo-maintenance pass. The through-line: the stand-ins become the things you run, and binary becomes graded.

  - **🧪 [Lab 48: Distributed backends and failure handling](labs/48-distributed-and-graded/)** makes the three Batch 77 signals production-shaped. `store.py` gains a `RedisStore` whose claim is one atomic Lua round-trip (cooldown via a per-key value, rate limit via a global sliding-window sorted set), so the claim holds across serverless and multi-region workers; `make_store('redis://...')` selects it behind the same `StateStore` interface (a SQL row-lock backend is the same shape). Every backend gains a `release`, and `notify.py` adds a `DeadLetter` queue so a send that exhausts its retries frees its cooldown slot and records the payload instead of silently keeping the slot. `canary.py` hashes normalized content (`normalize_corpus_text`), so a cosmetic reformat (line endings, trailing whitespace, blank-line runs) no longer triggers canary review. The nightly workflow passes `--dead-letter` and caches the queue.
  - **🧪 [Lab 49: Graded gold](labs/49-graded-gold/)** moves the rubric from binary to a 0-3 ordinal scale, measures agreement with Krippendorff's α (ordinal) and quadratic-weighted κ, adjudicates the items experts split on with a senior adjudicator rather than a silent majority, grades the judge (exposing a systematic one-level-low bias the binary gate hides), and re-derives the Lab 45 annotator weights against graded gold — where the ranking flips. Ships `graded_gold.jsonl` (three annotators + three experts + a senior label on split items + the judge, all on 0-3).

  Each lab ships the house structure (README brief, scaffold `lab.ipynb` with `TODO` cells, `solution/` with notes and reference notebook). Verified deterministically: `store.py --self-test` (in-memory cooldown/rate-limit + release; the naive claim races to 2, `FileLockStore` is atomic at 1; `RedisStore` over an in-process `FakeRedis` does claim + release + rate-limit); `notify.py --self-test` over the dict and shared-store paths plus a forced-failure case that dead-letters and frees the slot; `canary.py --self-test` (per-document review 5 vs 12, and normalized hashing maps a reformat to the same hash but a content edit to a different one); and for Lab 49, inter-expert ordinal α 0.90 above inter-annotator 0.86, the two gold protocols diverging on the three wide-split items, the graded judge at QWK 0.67 / MAE 0.54 / 46% off-by-one, and the annotator-weight ranking flipping from `a2,a3,a1` against the biased consensus to `a1,a2,a3` against gold. Ordinal α and quadratic-weighted κ are implemented inline (no `statsmodels`); `FakeRedis` mirrors Redis's single-threaded atomicity for the tests (production uses a real client and the same Lua). Citations are real (Redis `SET NX PX` / `EVAL`-Lua / sorted-set sliding window; the dead-letter-queue pattern; Krippendorff 1980 ordinal α; Cohen quadratic-weighted κ; Landis & Koch 1977); lab data is fictional, grounded in the Batch 71 corpus. The Lab 46 toolkit scripts are updated in place (additive; supersede the Batch 77 copies).

- **🧪 Scaling the RAG signals past a single point (Batch 77).** Two labs that remove the single-point assumptions the Batch 76 system still carries — one on each side — unified by a single discipline: any single point you trusted has to become plural and externally anchored before it holds at scale.

  - **🧪 [Lab 46: Scaling the signals across workers and traffic](labs/46-scaling-the-signals/)** scales the three Batch 76 signals for a fleet. A new `store.py` provides a `StateStore` with an atomic `try_claim` (in-memory for one process; `FileLockStore` over a shared filesystem, standing in for Redis `SETNX` / a DB row lock), and `notify.py`'s `deliver` now takes a store so exactly one worker pages for an incident — closing the check-then-record race — while staying backward compatible with Lab 44's dict-based calls. A new `build_reference.py` + `captured_traffic.jsonl` distill a larger clean, stratified, held-out reference from real traffic (with a confidence band that narrows as the sample grows), replacing the curated 16. `canary.py` gains a per-document fingerprint map so a corpus change flags only the canaries that depend on the changed docs (≈12 → the few affected). The three workflows are updated in place (shared store on the nightly notify, a cached per-document map on the drift check, a reference refresh before re-baselining on the loop).
  - **🧪 [Lab 47: Trustworthy gold](labs/47-trustworthy-gold/)** makes the evaluation anchor plural. A single-expert gold is just another biased annotator; this builds gold from multiple experts with agreement measured and splits adjudicated, and re-derives the Lab 40 judge ceiling against gold rather than consensus. Ships `expert_gold.jsonl` (three annotators + three experts + the judge per item).

- **🧪 Before-production hardening of the RAG signals and consensus (Batch 76).** Two labs that close the gaps the Batch 75 system shows the moment it meets real traffic — one on each side — unified by a single discipline: don't measure against yourself.

  - **`labs/44-hardening-the-signals/`** — the three Lab 42 signals, hardened. The notifier (`notify.py`) gains **retries** with backoff (a flaky webhook can't silently drop a page), a fixed-window **rate limit** (a regression storm can't become an alert storm), and **dedup/cooldown** keyed on `(metric, severity)` (the same incident can't re-fire while you're on it), with state persisted across runs via `--state-file`. The drift baseline (`record_baseline.py`) moves off the circular prototype trainset onto a **held-out clean reference** (`reference_sample.jsonl`, 16 realistic phrasings none verbatim in the trainset) — a model is over-confident on its own training data, so the prototype band sits too high and under-fires. The canary suite (`canary.py` + `canary_queries.jsonl`, grown to 16) is **tagged with named failure modes** (entity confusion, multi-hop shortcut, off-corpus overreach, paraphrase brittleness, stale fact, fabrication, route flip) and gains a **corpus-change review** (`corpus_fingerprint` + `review_status`) that flags exactly the corpus-dependent canaries to revalidate when the corpus hash changes. The three workflows are updated in place: the nightly persists cooldown state (`actions/cache` + `--state-file`), the drift check runs a non-blocking canary corpus-review step, and the maintenance loop records the baseline on the held-out reference.
  - **`labs/45-anchoring-the-consensus/`** — the evaluation-side twin. Lab 43 ranked annotators against the majority consensus, but the consensus is built from those same annotators, so the ranking is circular and a confidently wrong majority looks like truth. This adds a **gold** label (`annotations_with_gold.jsonl`, 20 items), shows the majority is collectively wrong on some items (consensus-vs-gold accuracy 0.90), re-ranks annotators against gold (an annotator reading 0.50 against consensus recovers to 0.70 against gold, because it was right where the majority was wrong), anchors reliability weights to gold, and spends adjudication on the disagreement queue plus a sample of agreements.

  Each lab ships the house structure (README brief, scaffold `lab.ipynb` with `TODO` cells, `solution/` with notes and reference notebook). Verified deterministically: `notify.py --self-test` (retry succeeds after two transient failures; cooldown suppresses a re-fire; rate limit blocks the 6th send in a 5/window cap; cooldown persists across runs); held-out band reads lower than the prototype band; canary failure-mode coverage + corpus-review flagging; nightly `scoring_items()` merges the grown 16-canary set; consensus-vs-gold 0.90 with the ranking shift on `a3`; all three workflows valid YAML with the hardening wired in. Navigation updated: Path 02 README (new Module 20) and `labs/README.md` catalog. The Lab 42 toolkit scripts are updated in place (additive; supersede the Batch 75 copies).

  References: Cohen's κ; Landis & Koch 1977; standard retry/backoff and rate-limiting patterns. The reference sample, grown canaries, and gold labels are fictional, grounded in the Batch 71 corpus.

- **🧪 Hardening the RAG operations and evaluation (Batch 75).** Two labs that close the gaps the Batch 74 system shows once it runs for real — one on the operations side, one on the evaluation side.

The notifier (`notify.py`) gains **severity** (a dip warns, a collapse pages, tuned by one `PAGE_FRACTION` knob) and **provider adapters** for Slack, PagerDuty, and GitHub issues, keeping the safe no-op default. `record_baseline.py` recomputes the drift baseline and is called by the promote phase, so `drift_check.py` (which now reads `confidence_baseline.json`) always compares live traffic against the current model. A fixed **canary** set (`canary.py` + `canary_queries.jsonl`) is folded into the drift window (`--canaries`) and the nightly scoring set, with a flipped canary route failing loudly regardless of traffic volume. The three Lab 41 workflows are updated in place: the drift check runs a hard canary routing check, the nightly notify step routes by severity to a real channel, and the maintenance loop's promote phase re-records both the gate thresholds (Lab 38) and the drift baseline (Lab 42).
  - **`labs/43-annotator-drift/`** — Lab 40 measured inter-annotator agreement once; this tracks each annotator's agreement-with-consensus across **rounds** (`annotation_rounds.jsonl`, three rounds with one drifting annotator), flags a drifting rater by trajectory (not a single noisy round), down-weights them in a reliability-weighted consensus, and recomputes the ceiling among the calibrated raters. The point: annotator drift and model drift are indistinguishable in a single judge-vs-consensus number, so only per-annotator tracking over time separates them.

  Each lab ships the house structure (README brief, scaffold `lab.ipynb` with `TODO` cells, `solution/` with notes and reference notebook). - **🧪 Running the RAG system for real (Batch 74).** Two labs that close Path 02's RAG track: know the evaluation's quality ceiling, and automate the upkeep loop around its human gates.

  - **`labs/40-annotation-quality/`** — Lab 38 validated the judge against one annotator; this adds three (`multi_annotator_labels.jsonl`) and measures **inter-annotator agreement** with Fleiss' κ (implemented inline, no `statsmodels`), the ceiling on how good any judge can look. It computes pairwise Cohen's κ to find the divergent annotator, builds a majority-vote consensus, scores the judge against it, and reports judge accuracy on clear vs ambiguous items separately. The lesson: a judge at the human ceiling is as good as the task allows; chasing past it fits annotation noise.
  - **`labs/41-operating-the-loop/`** — the operations layer over Labs 38–39, as three scripts and three workflows. A **drift trigger** (`drift_check.py` + `rag-drift-check.yml`) watches router confidence on live traffic and opens a retrain-due issue when it sags below the baseline band. A **notifier** (`notify.py`) turns a nightly faithfulness regression into a webhook alert, wired into `rag-faithfulness-nightly.yml` (which now also emits `faithfulness.json`). A **scheduled loop** (`run_loop.py` + `rag-maintenance-loop.yml`) runs the deterministic front half (capture → dedup → triage → review-queue artifact) and gates the promote half (retrain → measure → re-baseline) on a human review plus a measured lift. The stance: automate the deterministic steps, keep humans on the judgment steps.

  Each lab ships the house structure (README brief, scaffold `lab.ipynb` with `TODO` cells, `solution/` with notes and reference notebook). Verified deterministically: Fleiss' κ = 0.71 (3 annotators, 24 items) with judge-vs-consensus κ = 0.73 at the ceiling and judge accuracy 1.00 on the 19 unanimous items; pairwise Cohen's κ surfaces the divergent annotator; the three ops scripts' pure cores (`drift_status`, `should_notify`/`format_alert`/`post`, `triage_split`/`should_promote`) all pass `--self-test`; the notifier no-ops safely without a webhook; all three workflows are valid YAML (drift weekly with `issues:write`; maintenance-loop prepare/promote with a manual gate; nightly gains a notify step). - **🧪 Trusting and maintaining the RAG system (Batch 73).** Two labs that make the Batch 72 gate's numbers trustworthy and keep the router's training data fresh — the operational layer of Path 02's RAG track:

  - **`labs/38-calibrating-the-eval-gate/`** — three steps that turn the Lab 37 gate's outputs into trustworthy signals. (1) **Judge-vs-human agreement:** a hand-labeled validation set (`judge_validation.jsonl`, 24 triples) is scored by the LLM judge and compared with Cohen's κ, a confusion matrix, and a disagreement list, behind a trust rule (κ ≥ 0.60 → track the judge as a trend, never as ground truth). (2) **Thresholds from a baseline:** `derive_thresholds.py` writes `gate_thresholds.json` — a tolerance band below baseline for deterministic routing accuracy, mean − k·σ for noisy judged faithfulness — and Lab 37's `eval_gate.py` gains a `--thresholds` flag to read it. (3) **Operate, don't block:** `nightly_faithfulness.py` + a scheduled [workflow](.github/workflows/rag-faithfulness-nightly.yml) monitor judged faithfulness nightly (non-blocking), keeping the noisy metric off the PR path.
  - **`labs/39-router-data-lifecycle/`** — closes the loop on the Lab 36 router. The prototype trainset underrepresents messy real-user phrasing, shown by a sharp confidence drop on captured messy queries (`captured_queries.jsonl`, 36 simulated log queries). The lab dedups (string + embedding near-duplicate), triages by confidence to a human-review queue (active learning), merges reviewed labels, retrains, and **measures** the result on a held-out messy slice — promoting the new model only on a verified lift, then re-deriving the Lab 38 baseline. The lesson it enforces: a second training round is an experiment you measure, not a guaranteed win.

  Each lab ships the house structure (README brief, scaffold `lab.ipynb` with `TODO` cells, `solution/` with notes and reference notebook). Verified deterministically: judge-vs-human agreement math (accuracy/κ/confusion/trust rule); threshold derivation (deterministic tolerance band, noisy mean − k·σ) and the config-driven gate (baseline passes, regression fails, `_meta` ignored); both `--self-test` entrypoints; the nightly workflow YAML (scheduled, not PR-triggered); the distribution-shift signal (prototypes-only confidence 0.79 clean vs 0.40 messy with a TF-IDF stand-in), dedup, and confidence triage. The augmentation effect is presented as something to measure, not assume — the offline stand-in even shows a negative wobble on the tiny test slice, which the lab uses to make exactly that point. - **🧪 Production-hardening the adaptive router (Batch 72).** Two labs that take the Batch 71 router from a teaching prototype to a deployable, CI-gated component:

  - **`labs/36-training-the-router/`** — replaces Lab 35's per-query LLM classification with a **trained classifier**: a labeled query set (`router_trainset.jsonl`, 81 queries × 5 routes) → `sentence-transformers` embeddings → `LogisticRegression`, reported with 5-fold stratified cross-validation. Adds a **confidence gate** from `predict_proba` and an **agentic fallback** (`adaptive_rag_v2`) that, on low-confidence queries, tries the top-2 candidate strategies, self-checks each, and prefers a clean abstention over a failed guess. Compares trained vs prompt routing on accuracy and per-query cost. The lab flags the real risk as calibration, not accuracy.
  - **`labs/37-rag-eval-gates/`** — an **LLM-as-judge** scorer (`llm_judge` + defensive `parse_judge`, validated on adversarial fixtures where token-presence scoring fails) with the same signature as Lab 34's `answer_correct`, so it drops straight into the head-to-head harness; plus an **eval gate** wrapping the router. Ships a runnable [`eval_gate.py`](labs/37-rag-eval-gates/eval_gate.py) (routing-accuracy gate, deterministic, with a `--self-test` mode) and a [GitHub Actions workflow](.github/workflows/rag-eval-gate.yml) that blocks PRs on routing regressions. The design rule it teaches: block CI on cheap deterministic signals (routing accuracy), monitor expensive noisy ones (judged faithfulness) out of band.

  Each lab ships the house structure (README brief, scaffold `lab.ipynb` with `TODO` cells, `solution/` with notes and reference notebook). Verified deterministically: the labeled set is learnable and generalizes to Lab 34's held-out queries (15/16 with a TF-IDF stand-in for the embedder; the one miss is a genuinely borderline specific-vs-multihop paraphrase); the fallback's branch logic (top-passes / second-passes / prefer-abstain / low-confidence) is correct; the judge parser handles clean, fenced, embedded, and malformed JSON; `gate()` passes/fails/flags-missing correctly; `eval_gate.py --self-test` passes; the workflow YAML is valid. LLM- and embedding-dependent steps follow the repo's provider-agnostic client pattern. Navigation updated: Path 02 README (new Module 16) and `labs/README.md` catalog.

- **🧪 RAG pattern comparison, routing, and a dedicated Graph RAG corpus (Batch 71).** The synthesis of the SOTA-RAG arc — two new labs that measure and route the patterns from Batch 70, plus an entity-rich corpus that lets Graph RAG actually demonstrate its strengths:

  - **`labs/33-graph-rag-from-scratch/corpus/`** — a new, entirely fictional **entity-rich corpus** (a research ecosystem: researchers, labs, projects, publications, collaborations, funding). It is dense with recurring entities, explicit relationships, and genuine corpus-wide themes, so the graph is connected with detectable communities and multi-hop paths. Lab 33 now uses this corpus (`CORPUS_DIR = ./corpus`) instead of reusing Lab 06's concept corpus — Graph RAG needs entities and relationships to show value, which the concept corpus lacks. The Lab 33 README and notebooks are updated accordingly.
  - **`labs/34-rag-pattern-head-to-head/`** — runs static RAG, CRAG, Self-RAG, and Graph RAG over **one shared corpus and a shared 16-query eval set** (`eval_set.jsonl`, six categories), scoring them with the [evaluation framework](concepts/evaluation/rag-evaluation-framework.md): doc-level retrieval recall, answer correctness, and abstention correctness, sliced by category. The finding the lab is built to surface: each pattern wins the category it was designed for and none dominates — abstention scoring in particular exposes that static RAG fabricates on off-corpus queries.
  - **`labs/35-adaptive-rag-router/`** — a query classifier that routes each query to the category winner from Lab 34 (parametric → skip retrieval, global/multi-hop → graph, off-corpus-risk → CRAG, specific → flat). This realizes **Adaptive RAG** (Jeong et al. 2024) and is the synthesis of Labs 31–34. It reports routing accuracy and answer accuracy separately, and makes the cost argument (one classify call + one pattern vs running all four).

  Each lab ships the house structure (README brief, scaffold `lab.ipynb` with `TODO` cells, `solution/` with notes and reference notebook). Deterministic logic is verified: the corpus yields a connected 3-community graph with multi-hop paths; the head-to-head scoring discriminates patterns correctly (static scores 0 on off-corpus by fabricating, CRAG abstains and scores 1.0); the router's eval mapping covers every category and a correctly-routed query lands on a strategy that handles it. LLM-dependent steps follow the repo's provider-agnostic client pattern. The corpus is fictional; no real people, organizations, or papers are referenced.

- **🧪 SOTA RAG pattern labs (Batch 70).** Three from-scratch, runnable labs implementing the patterns introduced conceptually in Batch 69, each building on Lab 06's corpus and retrieval stack:

  - **`labs/31-corrective-rag-from-scratch/`** — CRAG: a retrieval evaluator that grades retrieval correct/ambiguous/incorrect, decompose-then-recompose knowledge refinement with citation provenance, and corrective actions (rewrite-and-retry, web-search fallback). Demonstrates routing an off-corpus query away from fabrication, plus grader calibration.
  - **`labs/32-self-rag-from-scratch/`** — Self-RAG: the reflection tokens (on-demand Retrieve decision, ISREL relevance, ISSUP support, ISUSE usefulness) approximated with constrained classification calls, per-passage candidate generation, and support+usefulness selection. The `chat_token` helper matches reflection tokens on word boundaries (so `irrelevant` is not mis-read as `relevant`).
  - **`labs/33-graph-rag-from-scratch/`** — Graph RAG: per-document entity/relationship extraction, a `networkx` knowledge graph, greedy-modularity community detection (a Leiden stand-in), community summarization, and both global (map-reduce over community summaries) and local (subgraph traversal) query paths with a router. Demonstrates the global-question win flat retrieval misses.

  Each lab ships the house structure: a `README.md` brief, a scaffold `lab.ipynb` with `TODO` cells, and a `solution/` (reference `lab.ipynb` + notes). All three out-of-sample mark their lab-grade simplifications versus the source papers — CRAG's evaluator is an LLM call rather than a fine-tuned model, Self-RAG's reflection tokens are prompt-approximated rather than fine-tuned, and Graph RAG uses greedy modularity rather than Leiden. - **📚 RAG evaluation and SOTA upgrade (Batch 69).** A research-grounded extension of the Path 02 (Agentic RAG) and Path 06 (Evaluation) material, plus new math, diagrams, recipes, and quizzes. This is an extend-and-consolidate batch: Path 02 was already mature (9 concept pages, 22 evaluation pages, 6 labs), so the work fills genuine gaps rather than rewriting. New surfaces:

  - **`concepts/rag/sota-rag-patterns.md`** — the modern RAG landscape (2024-2026): Self-RAG, Corrective RAG (CRAG), Adaptive RAG, agentic RAG, Graph RAG, long-context RAG, multimodal RAG (marked emerging), and structured-data RAG. Each pattern states what it adds over static RAG, when it earns its cost, and the real paper behind it, ending in a decision guide keyed to measured failures. Closes the page the `concepts/rag/README.md` previously forward-referenced as "coming."
  - **`concepts/evaluation/rag-evaluation-framework.md`** — a consolidated A-Z evaluation hub tying the four primer pages into one six-layer framework (eval set → component metrics → automated scoring → error taxonomy → CI/CD gates → production monitoring), with the full retrieval + generation metric taxonomy, an 8-row RAG error taxonomy, benchmark guidance (BEIR, MTEB), an `eval_gate` pseudocode block, and a week-by-week build order.
  - **`math-foundations/14-retrieval-ranking-metrics.md`** — precision@k, recall@k, MRR, MAP, NDCG, and the RAG-specific context precision/recall, with a worked numerical example whose values are verified against runnable Python (P@3 = 0.333, R@5 = 1.000, RR = 0.500, AP = 0.500, NDCG@5 = 0.618). Follows the Batch 68 math template (ASCII hyphens, clean LaTeX, code example, common mistakes, related pages). The first content page added since Path 08 v1 closed.
  - **`diagrams/rag-bundle.md`** + 9 `.mmd` source files — nine colorful, vertical (`flowchart TD`) Mermaid diagrams with `classDef` color tiers: basic and advanced pipelines, evaluation lifecycle, retrieval-vs-generation evaluation, agentic RAG, Graph RAG, production observability, failure diagnosis, and an evolution timeline. The color encodes stage/tier groupings (offline vs online, retrieval vs generation, era), so color carries information. All nine validated against the Mermaid parser.
  - **`recipes/rag/`** — opens the previously scaffold-only `recipes/` folder with a README and three runnable recipes: basic RAG, hybrid + reranked RAG (compact BM25 + RRF + cross-encoder rerank), and Corrective RAG (CRAG with a retrieval evaluator and fallback). Includes a local-models note for Ollama / OpenAI-compatible endpoints.
  - **`quizzes/agentic-rag/sota-rag-patterns.md`** and **`quizzes/math-foundations/retrieval-ranking-metrics.md`** — two 8-question quizzes (matching the existing structured-YAML quiz format) with answers and explanations, wired into Path 02 as Module 14's self-assessment.

- **🔧 Math foundations FULL REWRITE (Batch 68).** Every file in `math-foundations/` rewritten end-to-end with a richer template. The Batch 67 template (equation + intuition + engineering anchor + sources) gains five new section types: a "Why this matters for agentic AI" intro, a "How to read this equation" subsection, a minimal executable Python code example per page, a "Common mistakes" section, and an explicit "Related pages" cross-link block. Each reference now carries a one-sentence relevance note. Net effect: every math page is now self-contained for an engineer wanting to learn the concept, not just look up the equation.

  This is a continuous-improvement batch, not a v1-completion batch. Path 08 v1 closed in Batch 67; Batch 68 deepens the existing pages rather than adding new ones. The page count stays at 13; the per-page line count roughly doubles.

  **What was broken that this fixes**:
  - 20 occurrences of backslash-spacing-around-equals patterns across 11 files. These were intended as LaTeX thin-space spacing but rendered as visible semicolons on GitHub's KaTeX. The fix: drop the spacing commands around equals signs and use plain `=`. Where vertical spacing is genuinely needed, use `\quad` between equations or `aligned` inside `$$...$$`.
  - 209 em-dashes and en-dashes across the 15 files in `math-foundations/`. These render inconsistently across GitHub web, mobile, and editor previews. Replaced with ASCII hyphens throughout the math-foundations directory. (Convention applies only to files under `math-foundations/`; the rest of the repo retains its existing typographic conventions.)
  - Stale internal link in `notation.md` pointing at a renamed file. Fixed to the current canonical filename.

  **What each page gained**:
  - Page 01 (LM probability): perplexity computation snippet using OpenAI logprobs.
  - Page 02 (Embeddings): embed-and-rank corpus example using `text-embedding-3-small`.
  - Page 03 (RAG formulation): minimal RAG implementation showing the marginalization explicitly.
  - Page 04 (Agents as policies): the agent loop mapped to the equation, plus a POMDP belief-state section.
  - Page 05 (MDP / POMDP): toy MDP runner with state, action, reward.
  - Page 06 (ReAct): the thought-action factorization equation and a ReAct loop with structured logging.
  - Page 07 (Tool selection): tool calling with deliberately overlapping coverage to see selection in action.
  - Page 08 (Planning and search): plan-and-execute skeleton with bounded replanning.
  - Page 09 (Memory): three-tier `AgentMemory` class with importance-routed writes.
  - Page 10 (Multi-agent): supervisor-worker star topology with typed handoffs.
  - Page 11 (Evaluation): precision/recall/F1 + LLM-as-judge with structured outputs.
  - Page 12 (Uncertainty + safety): token entropy from logprobs + verbalized-confidence routing for HITL.
  - Page 13 (Context-window optimization): greedy knapsack + lost-in-the-middle reorder.

  Eleven new math pages (~80-120 lines each, ~1,100 lines total):

  - `math-foundations/01-language-model-probability.md` (~86 lines). The autoregressive next-token distribution $p(x_t \mid x_{<t};\, \theta)$; greedy / temperature / top-p decoding; reproducibility as a temperature decision; logprobs as diagnostics. Sources: Bengio 2003, Vaswani 2017, Hoffmann 2022 Chinchilla, Holtzman 2020 nucleus sampling.
  - `math-foundations/02-embeddings-vector-similarity.md` (~101 lines). Embedding as $\mathbf{u} = f_\phi(x) \in \mathbb{R}^d$; cosine similarity; lossy projection; cosine-vs-Euclidean; dimension matters less than expected; hybrid retrieval as production default. Sources: word2vec Mikolov 2013, Sentence-BERT Reimers 2019, DPR Karpukhin 2020, MTEB Muennighoff 2023.
  - `math-foundations/03-rag-formulation.md` (~100 lines). The marginalization $p(y \mid x) = \sum_z p(y \mid x, z) p(z \mid x)$; retriever vs generator separation; top-k as budget decision; agentic RAG as policy-over-retrieval. Sources: Lewis 2020 (RAG paper), REALM Guu 2020, Gao 2023 survey, Self-RAG Asai 2023.
  - `math-foundations/05-mdp-pomdp.md` (~98 lines). MDP tuple $(\mathcal{S}, \mathcal{A}, P, R, \gamma)$; Markov property as tractability assumption; POMDP belief state $b_t$; state design as engineering decision; belief-state vs policy failures. Sources: Sutton & Barto, Kaelbling 1998, Sumers 2024 cognitive architectures, Russell & Norvig.
  - `math-foundations/07-tool-selection.md` (~109 lines). Tool selection as sampling from $\pi_\theta$ over structured $\mathcal{A}$; tool surface size matters; description vs schema as separate controls; tool collision as canonical multi-tool failure. Sources: Toolformer Schick 2023, Gorilla Patil 2023, ToolLLM Qin 2023.
  - `math-foundations/08-planning-search.md` (~103 lines). Plan as sequence; search formulations (BFS / $A^*$ / MCTS); branching factor as difficulty driver; replanning as cheap search; plan-and-execute as production default. Sources: Russell & Norvig, ToT Yao 2023, Plan-and-Solve Wang 2023, RAP Hao 2023.
  - `math-foundations/09-memory-models.md` (~106 lines). Three-tier decomposition $M = M_\text{short} \cup M_\text{working} \cup M_\text{long}$; retrieval as RAG; write-side as half the problem; stale memory worse than missing memory; memory write decisions as attack surface. Sources: MemGPT Packer 2023, Generative Agents Park 2023, MemoryBank Zhong 2024, Mem0.
  - `math-foundations/10-multi-agent-coordination.md` (~118 lines). $G = (V, E)$ directed graph framing; topologies as graph constraints (star = supervisor-worker; tree = hierarchical; complete = swarm; chain = plan-execute); cycles need termination guards; handoff contracts as edges made explicit; coordination overhead superlinear in $|V|$. Sources: AutoGen Wu 2023, Generative Agents Park 2023, Russell & Norvig Ch. 17.5, Lynch *Distributed Algorithms*.
  - `math-foundations/11-evaluation-metrics.md` (~109 lines). Metric portfolio (classification + RAGAS faithfulness + LLM-as-judge + calibration ECE); offline vs online evaluation; judge ensemble for variance reduction; calibration as upstream of HITL triggering. Sources: RAGAS Es 2023, MT-Bench Zheng 2023, TruLens, Guo 2017 calibration.
  - `math-foundations/12-uncertainty-safety.md` (~113 lines). Entropy of $p(x_t \mid x_{<t})$; calibration as model+prompt property (not model alone); abstention as safety primitive (requires calibration to work); verbalized confidence as practical knob; hallucinations as confident wrong answers. Sources: Kadavath 2022, Guo 2017, Bommasani 2021 foundation models, Lin 2022 verbalized uncertainty.
  - `math-foundations/13-context-window-optimization.md` (~106 lines). Constrained selection (0/1 knapsack); greedy top-k as production approximation; lost-in-the-middle making ordering load-bearing; per-zone budgets prevent runaway growth; long-context models change the math without eliminating it. Sources: Liu 2023 lost-in-the-middle, Carbonell & Goldstein MMR 1998, Levy 2024 input-length impact, RULER Hsieh 2024.

  **Glossary cross-reference strategy**: rather than touching every concept page individually (71 files in `concepts/` alone), cross-references go in at the landing-page level. Readers landing in `concepts/`, `patterns/`, or `math-foundations/` see the glossary mentioned in their respective Cross-links / See also sections; readers landing in the glossary see arrow links out to canonical sources from every entry. This makes the glossary discoverable from any major content surface without requiring a per-file touch.

  **The ~65-entry initial size**: the glossary scaffold documents that entries are 5-minute PRs and new terms land continuously. The initial ~65 covers the core vocabulary across all eight substantial-content paths; future PRs from contributors will fill in domain-specific terms (per-framework terminology, security-specific attacks, eval-metric variants). The glossary is opened with sufficient coverage to be useful; it's not opened claiming to be comprehensive.

  **Each entry links to where the term is used canonically**: per the [`glossary/README.md`](./glossary/README.md) convention — "If a term has a precise mathematical meaning (e.g., *policy*, *belief state*), link to the corresponding `math-foundations/` page." Mathematical terms (Action, Belief state, MDP, POMDP, Policy) point at `math-foundations/`; pattern terms (Supervisor-worker, Swarm, Plan-and-execute, etc.) point at `patterns/`; concept terms point at `concepts/` subdirs; project-specific terms (Audit trail, Chunking, SEC EDGAR) point at the relevant project brief or path.

  **The Project 06 audit-trail schema as load-bearing engineering**: per Lawrence Emenike December 2025 framing — "audit trails allow a financial institution to reconstruct, explain, and defend every AI-driven decision when an auditor, regulator, or board member asks: 'How did this system arrive at that recommendation?'" Project 06's minimum schema (timestamp_UTC / audit_id / user_id / model_name+version / prompt_version / tool_calls / sources_cited / verdict+confidence) implements that reconstruction capability; the reproducibility dimension of the capstone-tier rubric verifies it. The regulations driving the requirement (SR 11-7 / NYDFS Part 500 / EU AI Act August 2026) are documented as constraint surface, not implementation surface — the project demonstrates the architecture, not regulated deployment.

  **The Project 08 chaos-test resilience as load-bearing engineering**: per LangChain April 2026 framing — "Durable execution is the foundation everything else depends on." Project 08's chaos-test suite (mid-run server restart / network partition during retrieval / cost-cap firing / 30+ minute HITL pause / all-of-the-above sequenced) verifies that the durability layers actually work together; the "4 of 5 tests pass" target separates capstone-tier from intermediate-tier. The two-layer architecture decision (LangGraph-only vs LangGraph + Temporal vs Deep Agents runtime) is the ADR-bearing choice; the "checkpoints between nodes, not inside" critical limitation drives the checkpoint-granularity decisions.

  **The Intermediate tier as a complete tier**: Path 09 v1 now has all three intermediate-tier project briefs (#03 Project management agent + #04 Data analysis agent + #05 Multi-server MCP agent). Each emphasizes a different production surface — multi-agent topology + MCP integration (#03); code-as-action + silent-error defense (#04); multi-server tool composition (#05). Together they cover the three canonical intermediate-tier patterns engineers ship in 2026; choosing among them is a matter of which production surface maps to the reader's role or domain.

  **The silent-error defense as Project 04's load-bearing engineering challenge**: per the Decodesfuture January 2026 framing — silent errors (wrong-answer-with-high-confidence) are the canonical 2026 failure mode for data-analysis agents. Project 04 documents three defenses (profiling-on-ingestion / self-skepticism prompt / verifiable citations) and verifies them with a deliberate test suite (5 questions where the naive answer is wrong: missing-data masquerade / outlier dominance / confounder confound / survivorship bias / Simpson's paradox). Milestone 5's "3+ of 5 cases flagged" target is the load-bearing capstone-tier verification specific to this project.

  **The three failure modes as Project 05's load-bearing engineering challenge**: per the Getknit April 2026 framing — when 3+ MCP servers are in play, three failure modes emerge that aren't present in single-server tutorials (tool name collision / ambiguous routing / unclear failure attribution). Project 05 documents three defenses (namespace tools at agent layer / per-tool metadata in system prompt / per-server trace baggage) and verifies them with a fault-injection test suite. The "2 of 3 fault tests pass" target is the load-bearing intermediate-tier verification specific to this project.

  **The ReAct + code interpreter architecture in Project 04**: per the Together.ai reference architecture — "the agent will first 'think' and then 'act'. Each action the agent generates is a Python snippet (e.g., `import pandas as pd; pd.read_csv(...)`)". This is strongly inspired by smolagents, CodeAct, and the original ReAct paper. The pattern grants the agent maximum flexibility against tabular data; pre-built tools that expect specific schemas can't match it. Sandbox choice (E2B / Together Code Interpreter / Riza / local Docker) is the ADR-bearing decision; the silent-error defense is the engineering depth.

  **The one-client-per-server pattern in Project 05**: per the Skyvern May 2026 architecture explainer — "When a host connects to multiple servers simultaneously (for example, a filesystem server and a GitHub server at the same time) it spawns a separate client instance for each one. This one-to-one mapping keeps connections isolated." Project 05 implements this pattern explicitly: the agent is the MCP host; it spawns one client per consumed server; the connection pool managed lifecycle is exercised in Milestone 3.

  Two new files:
  - `concepts/context/context-drift-detection.md` (~24 min reading, 241 lines). Module 5 content. The four early-warning signals: re-reads (same tool call hash appearing in N+3 turns), re-decisions (structured-output stability degrading), task reframing (cosine distance from turn-1 framing growing), retrieval-precision collapse (relevance scores trending downward over conversation). Signal hierarchy with leading-vs-lagging classification — re-reads and re-decisions are leading (5-10 turn lead time before quality failure); task reframing and retrieval-precision collapse are lagging. Trace-level OpenTelemetry instrumentation per signal with span attributes named and a reference instrumentation snippet using `args_hash` for Signal 1 detection. Reusing Path 06 v2 Lab 23 infrastructure — centroid shift / cosine-distance distribution shift / nearest-neighbor overlap / per-cluster rate-of-change all transfer from RAG-pipeline-level drift detection to conversation-level context drift. Detection thresholds with per-conversation-type baselines (2σ deviation triggers alerts on leading signals; composite firing across 3+ signals triggers intervention). Three intervention shapes: force compaction / escalate to fresh session / escalate to human. Five operational practices, three anti-patterns. 7 dated 2026 references.

  **The Module 5 four-signal vocabulary as the load-bearing detection framing**: each signal points at a specific failure mode the agent's reasoning loop produces when its context window stops working. Signal 1 (re-reads) detects compression/memory failure; Signal 2 (re-decisions) detects persistent state not being read; Signal 3 (task reframing) is the compounded effect of 1 + 2; Signal 4 (retrieval-precision collapse) is the lagging consequence. The signal hierarchy is what makes alerting strategy possible — alert on leading signals (1, 2); monitor lagging signals (3, 4); auto-escalate composite firing.

  **The Module 5 Lab 23 → Module 5 mapping as the operational reuse pattern**: the four Lab 23 detector mechanics (centroid shift, cosine-distance distribution shift, nearest-neighbor overlap, per-cluster rate-of-change) transfer from RAG-pipeline-level drift to conversation-level context drift with no code changes. Same statistics, different time horizons. The repo's drift-detection investment compounds: Lab 20 covers score-side drift (output), Lab 23 covers embedding-space drift (RAG-pipeline-level input), Module 5 covers context drift (conversation-level input). Three detectors, one telemetry substrate.

  **The Module 6 advertised-vs-effective context distinction as the 2026 load-bearing framing**: per Digital Applied April 2026, "Marketing claims of 1M-token context windows hide a 30-60 point retrieval drop between 200K and 1M for every frontier model except Gemini 3 Deep Think. The phrase '1M context' on a model card is a capacity statement; it is not a quality statement." The framing reorients the design decision from "what's the largest window I can buy?" to "what's the effective context for this workload?" — which depends on whether the workload is single-needle or multi-needle, and on which frontier model is being used.

  **The Module 6 MRCR v2 8-needle finding as the practical anchor**: Claude Sonnet 4.5 scores ~88% on single-needle NIAH at 1M but 18.5% on MRCR v2 8-needle at 1M. Claude Opus 4.6 scores ~78% on the same MRCR v2 8-needle test — a roughly quadruple improvement. The 60-point gap between Sonnet 4.5's single-needle and multi-needle scores at the same context length is the canonical illustration. Production deployments past ~400K effective context typically pair long-context with explicit retrieval rather than relying on the model's internal retrieval.

  **The Module 6 Claude Opus 4.7 flat-pricing shift as the 2026 economics anchor**: Claude Opus 4.7 shipped 1M context at flat pricing in April 2026 (no surcharge above 200K). This decoupled the pricing-vs-quality question from the pricing-vs-quantity question and changed the cost calculus for long-context workloads. The Module 4 crossover (long-context vs tiered memory for <500K accumulated single-user history) becomes more attractive at the Opus 4.7 economics; the decision matrix in Module 6 updates the crossover with the effective-context findings layered on top.

  **The Module 3 anchored iterative pattern as the load-bearing production discipline**: full-reconstruction summarization (regenerate from scratch each cycle) produces compounding information loss; anchored iterative (merge new turns into a persistent summary state) caps the loss at the first cycle and stabilizes from there. Factory's 36,000-session evaluation per Zylos showed anchored iterative outperforming full-reconstruction on accuracy, completeness, and continuity. The structured prompt that explicitly lists categories to preserve (identifiers, open questions, user-stated constraints) is what makes the anchor work; generic "summarize the above" prompts drift.

  **The Module 3 four-strategy framing as the decision vocabulary**: truncation drops content (no recovery); summarization replaces with generated summary (lossy but preserves structure); masking changes attention without changing prefix (no token reduction but preserves KV cache reuse, per Manus 2025); context folding aggressively reduces via sub-trajectory summaries (most aggressive, best for long-horizon agents). The choice depends on what the zone is for, not on a single best answer. Conversation history of a resolved customer-support ticket benefits from truncation; conversation history of a multi-step research investigation needs summarization or context folding.

  **The Module 4 three-tier vocabulary as the cross-session architecture**: short-term (the context window itself) / long-term (persistent across sessions, vector / graph / SQL backends per dimension) / episodic (past complete experiences). The session boundary divides short-term from long-term; the granularity (facts vs experiences) divides long-term from episodic. The four-memory-dimensions framing (semantic similarity / multi-hop / temporal / cross-session coherence) names the trade-off space: no single backend solves all four, which is why production-mature systems combine vector + graph + SQL per dimension.

  **The Claude Opus 4.7 1M-token crossover as the 2026 economics shift**: per Digital Applied May 2026, the flat-pricing change (no surcharge above 200K) makes long-context cost-competitive with tiered memory for single-user / small-fleet deployments with <500K accumulated tokens and <10 sessions. The crossover is an economics decision, not a capability gap — large context windows delay but do not fix memory failures per Atlan April 2026. For multi-user / large-fleet cases, tiered memory's per-user retrieval cost stays roughly constant while long-context scales linearly with user count.

  **The Module 4 LangChain memory deprecation as the 2026 build-phase footgun**: BufferMemory, ConversationBufferMemory, ConversationSummaryBufferMemory, VectorStoreRetrieverMemory from `langchain.memory` all deprecated as of 2026 per Digital Applied. LangGraph's checkpointer-based short_term + long_term pattern is the only officially supported approach. Many published tutorials still reference the deprecated APIs — teams following older guides build on sand. The Module 4 anti-scope explicitly flags this rather than attempting a full migration walkthrough.

  **The two-threshold soft cap / hard cap pattern as the production discipline**: Module 2's central operational pattern. Soft cap triggers compression before degradation; hard cap converts silent failures (suicide by context) into explicit errors the agent's retry layer can handle. The code example shows zone-aware compressors (history-summarization vs retrieval-reranking vs tool-filtering); the manager lives at the harness layer per the Agent Harness Engineering survey (CMU + Yale + JHU + others, 2026).

  **The Module 6 four-layer policy/defense/evaluation/red-team structure as the load-bearing framing**: a policy gap produces inconsistent enforcement; a defense gap produces incidents; an evaluation gap produces silent regressions; a red-team gap produces undiscovered failures. All four are independent failure modes; the page is explicit that authoring this one (policy) doesn't address the others, but the others can't be addressed without it. The traceability table connecting policy entry → defense layer → evaluation test → red-team probe is the EU AI Act Article 9 + 11 + 17 + 26 mapping in concrete form.

  **The Module 6 cross-vertical pattern as the generalizable structure**: medical / legal / financial verticals all follow the same three-tier structure (hard refusal as regulatory red line / escalation as qualified-human handoff / allowed as general information), but the *boundary location* per tier is vertical-specific and requires domain-expert authorship. The engineering work is in making the boundaries explicit enough for implementation; the policy work is in placing them correctly. The Inverse Risk Calibration finding ties policy authorship to actual deployment risk — weight refusal criteria by severity × likelihood (Expected Harm), not severity alone.
