- **🚀 Path 06 v2 second batch — Production Patterns (Batch 34).** The second Path 06 v2 batch ships three reusable production evaluation/observability patterns plus an index and template, complementing the Batch 33 recipes. The framing carries forward: **recipes** are end-to-end deployment compositions (which modules go in what order for a specific stack); **patterns** are cross-cutting mechanisms applied *inside* any recipe. Same Path 06 v2 family, different artifact shape.

  A team running the Hybrid recipe (Recipe 3) doesn't just install LangSmith + OTel — they also need a retrieval policy that respects cost budgets per tenant, a drift workflow that routes signals to human reviewers rather than blind retraining, and an evaluator combination strategy that handles judge disagreements. Those mechanisms are the patterns this batch ships.

  **Design choice — path-scoped at `learning-paths/06-evaluation-observability/patterns/`, not in the top-level `/patterns/` directory**: top-level `/patterns/` catalogs architecture patterns for agentic systems (single-agent, router, supervisor + workers, plan-and-execute, swarm, reflection, agentic RAG, deep research, HITL, MCP, A2A) — classified as **stable** under an 8-section template. Path 06 v2 patterns are a different category — production evaluation/observability mechanisms applied inside recipes — and live with the other Path 06 v2 work. Same path-scoping logic as Batch 33 used for recipes. The two `/patterns/` directories are orthogonal: an agentic-RAG topology (top-level pattern 08) can use cost-aware retrieval (Path 06 pattern 01) and judge ensemble (Path 06 pattern 03) without conflict.

  - `learning-paths/06-evaluation-observability/patterns/README.md` — Patterns index. Brief framing (recipes vs patterns vs concept pages vs labs); decision tree (mermaid) for picking between the three patterns; explanation of how patterns relate to v1 modules; how patterns differ from architecture patterns in the top-level directory; pointer to the template. Establishes "production evaluation mechanisms" as the v2 pattern category.

  - `learning-paths/06-evaluation-observability/patterns/01-cost-aware-retrieval.md` (~12 min) — Intent: adapt retrieval decisions (k, reranking, web fallback, agentic loop) by a four-input policy (tenant_tier, task_value, remaining_budget, retrieval_confidence). Module 6 anchor (cost attribution + adaptive sampling). Mechanism: a retrieval policy function that consumes baggage + budget + confidence signals and returns a `RetrievalSettings` dataclass. Three-tier defaults (free/pro/enterprise) with confidence-based escalation when budget headroom allows. Anti-scope: not a replacement for relevance evaluation; not a substitute for offline eval per tier. Implementation sketch is ~30 lines including the policy table. Grounding: Kalviumlabs May 2026 (k=3 → k=8 is ~100× cost increase); MarsDevs May 2026 (CRAG-style retrieval-evaluator confidence threshold pattern); Lushbinary April 2026 (Hybrid+Rerank as production default).

  - `learning-paths/06-evaluation-observability/patterns/02-drift-triggered-review.md` (~14 min) — **Intent: when drift signals fire, route to a human review queue with severity-tiered urgency — do NOT auto-retrain by default.** Module 5 anchor (drift detection + judge calibration). The pattern's central insight: auto-retrain on drift is the wrong default — the new model covers up the diagnostic signal instead of resolving it, and silently introduces a different kind of mistake nobody catches until users complain. Three-tier response policy: T1 mild (KS p ∈ [0.001, 0.05]) → annotation queue, weekly review, no paging; T2 moderate (KS p < 0.001 sustained 24h) → page eval engineer, investigate by next business day; T3 severe (eval pass-rate ↓ > 10pp) → page on-call, deploy fallback, same-day investigation. The retraining decision is downstream of investigation, never automatic from a signal. Frames the pattern as Human-on-the-Loop (HOTL) — automated routine + defined risk triggers, per Kili Technology Feb 2026's HITL vs HOTL distinction. Grounding: Label Your Data Sept 2025 (tiered drift response framing); Braintrust April 2026 platform survey (confirms ongoing calibration workflows are an underdeveloped gap in current platforms).

  - `learning-paths/06-evaluation-observability/patterns/03-judge-ensemble.md` (~15 min) — Intent: when LLM-as-judge calibration accuracy matters (launches, win-rate decisions, near-noise-band comparisons), combine multiple judges from different model families. Three combination strategies: majority vote (simple, fails on correlated bias per AgentAuditor Feb 2026); weighted vote (per-judge κ from calibration data; reduces correlated-bias problem); disagreement routing (split verdicts → human queue, reserves human bandwidth for hard cases). Module 4 + Module 5 anchors. Production-realistic mid-2026 default per FutureAGI May 2026: 3-judge ensemble across Claude Sonnet 4.5 + GPT-5.1 + Gemini 2.5 Pro for high-stakes work; single judge with calibration for weekly trends. 3× cost; family-specific biases cancel; reserve for the launch-decision subset. Anti-scope: not a replacement for rubric quality (a bad rubric defeats any ensemble); not the AgentAuditor reasoning-tree adjudication (different more-sophisticated pattern). Implementation sketch covers all three combination strategies in ~35 lines.

The framing: v1 documents the modules in isolation (one module per concept page; one lab per module); v2 documents the compositions — given a team's existing stack, which modules apply, in what order, with what hand-off discipline.

  - `learning-paths/06-evaluation-observability/recipes/01-langsmith-native.md` (~20 min) — For teams already on LangChain/LangGraph; fastest zero-to-production; vendor coupling traded for the LLM-eval UX. Uses Modules 1, 2, 4 (LangSmith side), 5, 7. Step sequence: env-var auto-tracing + `@traceable` for helpers → LangSmith Datasets versioned eval sets → Automation Rules for online evaluation → Dataset diff for drift review → annotation queues for human calibration → threads for multi-turn. Lab-shape vs production-shape table contrasts the lab's hand-rolled SDK polling against production's UI-defined Automation Rules. Cost envelope: $44-54/mo at 10K traces, $89-228/mo at 100K, custom Enterprise at 1M. Anti-scope: cost attribution beyond LangSmith built-ins; tail-based sampling; vendor-neutral telemetry.

  - `learning-paths/06-evaluation-observability/recipes/02-opentelemetry-native.md` (~22 min) — For teams with existing observability stack (Datadog, Honeycomb, self-hosted Tempo+Grafana); agents shouldn't be an observability island. Uses Modules 1, 3, 4 (Collector side), 5, 6, 7. Step sequence: OTel SDK with GenAI semantic conventions (`gen_ai.*`) → Collector with tail-sampling + cost-driven policies (Lab 19 + Lab 21 YAMLs) → baggage propagation for cost attribution → evaluators against the trace stream (worker process or backend-native) → drift detection on the metrics pipeline → multi-turn scores as span attributes. Documents two evaluator patterns (Pattern A: streaming worker service; Pattern B: backend-native via Datadog LLM Obs / Honeycomb / MLflow). Lab-shape vs production-shape table contrasts SimpleSpanProcessor + ConsoleSpanExporter against BatchSpanProcessor + OTLPSpanExporter. Cost envelope: ~$420-800/mo at 100K (Datadog-backed); ~$70-150/mo at 100K (self-hosted Tempo+Grafana). Anti-scope: LLM-eval UX; out-of-the-box trajectory evaluators; easy migration to LangSmith later.

  - `learning-paths/06-evaluation-observability/recipes/03-hybrid-langsmith-and-otel.md` (~25 min) — **The central recipe of the batch.** For production teams needing both LLM-eval UX and vendor-neutral telemetry; the most realistic mid-2026 production shape per Digital Applied April 2026 industry-survey data ("most teams pick one primary platform and pair it with a whole-stack APM"). Uses **all seven** modules with explicit hand-off discipline. The recipe's central artifact is the hand-off-discipline section documenting which artifact is emitted by what component, consumed by what destination, lives where, and which view is the source of truth when scores live in both places (operational truth in APM; evaluation truth in LangSmith; replicate when both views need read access). Step sequence: dual-emit OTel SDK with `langsmith.*` hint attributes → Collector with two-pipeline shape (APM gets everything sampled, LangSmith gets a further-filtered eval-relevant subset) → eval-subset policy (100% errors + 100% slow + 100% high-cost + 20% baseline) → LangSmith eval UX layer → APM operational layer → score replication worker. **Operationally feasible only after LangChain's March 2026 announcement of end-to-end OTel support in LangSmith SDK ≥ 0.4.25**; what was custom plumbing 12 months ago is now a documented integration. Cost envelope: $405-834/mo at 100K, $2800-6100/mo at 1M; subset routing math drops LangSmith ingestion costs ~80% vs sending everything. Anti-scope: a simpler deployment than Recipes 1 or 2; vendor independence end-to-end (still committed to LangSmith for eval UX).

Documents the `@traceable`-for-the-application-surface / `RunTree`-for-explicit-tree-manipulation choice; `agentevals` ready-made evaluators over hand-rolled; sync evaluators for clarity (async = `aevaluate` pattern reference); project scoping via `tracing_v2_enabled` as the dev/CI separation lever; the Lab 16 custom-evaluator integration showing any `(run, example) -> {key, score}` callable is a valid LangSmith evaluator.
  - `labs/18-opentelemetry-portable-tracing/solution/{README.md, lab.ipynb}` — A vendor-neutral OTel-instrumented supervisor agent (same agent as Lab 17, instrumentation moved from LangSmith-native to OpenTelemetry GenAI semantic conventions). Fanout exports to LangSmith + console + (optional) Jaeger from a single `TracerProvider`. Documents the `SimpleSpanProcessor` (lab) vs `BatchSpanProcessor` (production) choice; fanout via multiple `SpanProcessor` instances on the same `TracerProvider`; GenAI semantic conventions (`gen_ai.*` prefix) over vendor-specific names; `OpenAIInstrumentor` for auto-instrumentation + manual `gen_ai.invoke_agent` span for agent-level operations; `SpanKind.CLIENT` for LLM calls.
  - `labs/19-online-evaluation-and-sampling/solution/{README.md, lab.ipynb}` — Two halves. Half A: synthetic-trace generation + reference-free evaluator + SDK polling pattern + sample-rate cost arithmetic. Half B: production-realistic Collector YAML + priority-policy stack simulated in Python + two-tier topology rationale. Documents SDK polling over LangSmith Rules for portability; synthetic traces over real agent runs (the lab is about online-eval infrastructure, not agent behavior); reference-free evaluators as the canonical online-eval pattern; `decision_wait=30s` + `num_traces=360000` as the agent-specific defaults; probabilistic-within-tail rather than upstream probabilistic.
  - `labs/20-drift-detection-and-calibration/solution/{README.md, lab.ipynb}` — Two halves. Half A: three drift-detection algorithms (KS-test, PSI from scratch, Wasserstein) on three drift patterns (gradual, abrupt, shape-only) + rolling-window stream detector with ~50-sample latency. Half B: simulated LLM-as-judge against a 10-example human gold set; verbosity-bias mitigation moving κ from 0.000 → 1.000; 12-week calibration drift simulation. Documents PSI implemented from scratch (visible binning math, 0.1/0.25 thresholds); three drift patterns chosen to expose which test misses what; rolling-window over per-sample-test; 10-example gold set as the smallest statistically meaningful calibration anchor; length-controlled mitigation as the canonical bias-removal lever; the 3-week detection delay as the false-positive-rate vs latency trade-off.
  - `labs/21-cost-attribution-and-adaptive-sampling/solution/{README.md, lab.ipynb}` — Real OTel SDK with `ConsoleSpanExporter` + `SimpleSpanProcessor`. Half A: baggage propagation through planner → tool-caller → synthesizer agent; four token layers; 200-trace burn-down. Half B: Collector YAML + `simulate_policies` + `AdaptiveSamplingController` + two-tier topology + cost arithmetic. **Verified to produce identical canonical demonstration numbers as the source lab** (acme-corp 65.1% of spend, analysis heaviest task at $0.058/trace avg, 88% ingestion-cost reduction at 12% retention) — condensation preserved behavior exactly.

The three shifts from 2024 → 2026 that moved multi-turn from optional to required (agents replaced single-shot prompts; production traces revealed the cross-turn failure gap; thread-level tracing went mainstream). The four canonical conversation-level metrics with operational definitions: Conversation Completeness (the single most important; extract intents → check satisfaction), Knowledge Retention (extract facts → detect re-asks), Role Adherence (per-turn LLM-as-judge against role spec), Turn Relevancy (response appropriate given full prior history). The conversation-level vs turn-level vs trajectory distinction with the O(n × k) framing for trajectory evaluation (every tool call is its own evaluation surface — a coding agent making 15 tool calls per task generates 15 checkpoints). Threads-as-first-party (LangSmith October 2025 release; the pattern that spread to DeepEval, Confident AI, MLflow GenAI, Langfuse). The LangChain Deep Agents five-pattern framework for production agent evaluation (bespoke test logic; single-step evals; full agent turn testing; multi-turn with conditional logic; proper environment setup). Span-attached scores as the operational pattern for CI/production parity. The three persona archetypes with system-prompt sketches and the failure modes each catches: cooperative (provides info upfront, accepts answers — catches baseline task-completion failures), distracted / chaotic (changes topics, contradicts itself — catches knowledge-retention failures), adversarial (probes role boundaries under cover — catches role-adherence and security failures). The 50/30/20 traffic-distribution argument and the cooperative-only trap as the silent killer of simulation suites (a 100% cooperative suite covers ~60-70% of production traffic and misses the 30-40% that breaks the agent in real life). The production tools landscape as of mid-2026: DeepEval `ConversationSimulator` (OSS), MLflow `ConversationSimulator` (experimental in 3.10), LivePerson Conversation Simulator (enterprise, EU AI Act / NIST AI RMF audit-ready, Telstra deployment). The sliding-window pattern for long conversations with the cross-window-contradiction trade-off. The persona-consistency problem (LLMs simulating users drift toward generic-helpful behavior past turn 10-12, documented across Zhang 2018, Ge 2024, Paglieri 2026, Wang 2025) with three mitigations (short conversations, explicit consistency instructions, RL-fine-tuned simulator models as research frontier). The Sim2Real gap (Zhou et al. 2026) and why simulation supplements rather than replaces production-trace evaluation. - **Path 06 Module 6 — Cost attribution + adaptive sampling (Batch 29).** Fifth Path 06 lab. The production-operations closer that ties Modules 1-5 to unit economics: attribute cost to tenants/users/tasks via OTel baggage propagation; then adapt sampling decisions to per-tenant burn rate. With Module 6 shipped, the Path 06 production stack covers all five operational layers: instrumentation (M2-3), online evaluation (M4), drift detection (M5), calibration (M5), and cost attribution + adaptive sampling (M6). Module 6 is the sixth of seven planned modules.
  - `concepts/evaluation/cost-attribution.md` (~14 min) — The three attribution dimensions with their distinct product questions: per-tenant (B2B unit economics), per-user (cohort analysis, abuse detection), per-task (engineering optimization). The four token layers (prompt, tool, memory, response) and why aggregating to input/output hides where spend goes; each layer has distinct optimization machinery (prompt caching, tool-spec compression, RAG tuning). The day-one instrumentation rule with the ~5x retrofit cost from production case studies. OTel baggage as the propagation primitive: what it is (a propagation mechanism, not a telemetry signal), the W3C 4KB total limit, the Python API (`baggage.set_baggage`, `context.attach`, `baggage.get_baggage`) shown with concrete code. The four discipline rules: IDs only / no PII / no secrets / allowlist at ingest. The "set baggage early, set span attributes redundantly" pattern (baggage propagates; span attributes are searchable; both are required). The three-layer enforcement ladder: Layer 1 dashboards (per-dimension burn-down, daily refresh), Layer 2 alerts (per-tenant vs 7-day baseline, 2x warning, 5x critical), Layer 3 rate-limit tightening (per-tenant token bucket pending review). Incremental rollout argument: premature Layer-3 is the canonical "auto-throttled a real customer's legitimate burst" failure mode. Pricing-tier propagation as the cost-attribution-to-sampling bridge. Six references including Digital Applied's April 2026 production guide (the three-dimension framing and 5x retrofit cost), SoftwareSeni's five-element governance pattern, OneUptime's baggage Python API patterns and W3C wire format.
  - `concepts/evaluation/adaptive-sampling.md` (~12 min) — Cost-driven policies in the `tailsamplingprocessor`: `numeric_attribute` on `gen_ai.cost.total_usd` with `min_value` threshold (keep all traces above 10¢); `string_attribute` on `tenant.tier` (keep enterprise traces preferentially). A canonical production priority stack: errors → latency → high-cost → enterprise → probabilistic-baseline. The probabilistic-within-tail pattern with the upstream OTel rationale (avoid dropping high-priority traces before the tail policies can match). The external control loop with mermaid diagram and two concrete strategies: (1) sampling rate inversely proportional to remaining budget (with quadratic falloff code); (2) adaptive thresholds on policy triggers (raise the high-cost threshold when traffic spikes). Three push mechanisms: file-watching reload, OPAMP protocol (GA in 2026 for fleet-scale remote config), sidecar with remote-config endpoint. The two-tier Collector topology with mermaid: tier 1 `loadbalancingexporter` with `routing_key: traceID`, tier 2 `tailsamplingprocessor` on the sticky subset; the half-trace failure mode that argues for it. Buffer sizing formula `num_traces ≥ traces_per_sec × decision_wait × 1.2` with the agent-specific decision_wait of 30s (default 10s is wrong for agents with multi-second tool calls). The cost-control runbook: dashboard the sampling rate, monitor `sampling_trace_dropped_too_early`, alert on retention SLO violations, replay before policy changes. Six references including the upstream OTel `tailsamplingprocessor` README (canonical policy types, two-tier topology recommendation, probabilistic-within-tail guidance), OneUptime's February 2026 series on tail-sampling configuration and probabilistic cost control, Chaos To Clarity's dynamic-threshold pattern.

  Path 06 v1 status: Modules 1-6 shipped. The full production-operations story is now documented end-to-end. Module 7 (multi-turn evaluation) closes Path 06 v1 in 1-2 future batches.

- **Path 06 Module 5 — Drift detection + agent-as-judge calibration (Batch 28).** Fourth Path 06 lab. The trust-loop closer that sits on top of Module 4: detect when evaluator scores drift over time (KS-test, PSI, Wasserstein, rolling-window monitoring); calibrate LLM-as-judge against periodic human ground truth (Cohen's kappa, five named biases and their mitigations). Without these, drift detection on uncalibrated scores produces alerts you can't act on; with them, the Path 06 trust stack is complete. Module 5 is the fifth of seven planned modules.
  - `concepts/evaluation/drift-detection.md` (~14 min) — The three flavors of LLM drift in 2026: prompt drift (your changes), model drift (provider silent weight updates — the GPT-4 Turbo example), eval-score drift (the detectable signal). Why classical ML drift detection doesn't fully map: LLM evaluator scores are already aggregations, not raw features; the drift to catch is on the score stream, not on input features (though embedding-space input drift is a complementary signal). The four canonical statistical tests with concrete scipy/numpy code: `ks_2samp` (continuous, nonparametric, shape-sensitive), PSI from first principles (binned, interpretation-friendly with the <0.1 stable / 0.1-0.25 moderate / >0.25 significant thresholds widely used across financial ML), `wasserstein_distance` (scale-aware, in score units), `chisquare` (categorical). Decision table for which test answers which drift question. Rolling-window pattern with three windows (baseline frozen / reference sliding / current). The alerting problem and the production pattern: two-tier warning + critical thresholds, persistence requirements (N consecutive windows before alerting), slice dashboards, proxy-signal confirmation, runbook gating. Monitoring without labels via proxy signals (confidence distributions, abstain rates, output length, retrieval-rank distributions, delayed-label backfill). Tool landscape: Evidently (OSS Python, CI-friendly), NannyML (performance without ground truth), whylogs/WhyLabs (privacy-preserving profile-based), Arize Phoenix (OTel-native embedding drift), FutureAGI (full agent stack). The silent-provider-weight-update failure mode with the GPT-4 Turbo motivation case. Six references including All Days Tech's 2026 production-first runbook, Galileo's 9-platform comparison, FutureAGI's three-flavor LLM drift taxonomy, Galtea's 2026 evaluation guide.
  - `concepts/evaluation/agent-as-judge-calibration.md` (~13 min) — Five named LLM-judge biases circa 2026: **position bias** (≤0.04 in newer instruction-tuned models; was 10-15pp in MT-Bench era), **verbosity bias** (longer answers score higher in both pairwise and pointwise — Saito et al. 2023; Wu & Aji 2024), **self-preference bias** (10-25pp boost for own-family outputs — Panickssery et al. 2024), **format/style bias** (judges prefer bulleted/markdown — Wu & Aji 2024), **calibration drift** (judge scoring distribution shifts as the underlying model updates). Mitigations that survive production: permutation averaging for position bias, length-controlled prompts and post-hoc length-controlled win rates (Dubois et al. 2024) for verbosity, cross-family judging for self-preference, format-normalization for format bias, periodic kappa-against-gold-set for calibration drift. Cohen's kappa with the Landis & Koch 1977 interpretation ranges (<0 broken / 0-0.20 slight / 0.20-0.40 fair / 0.40-0.60 moderate / 0.60-0.80 substantial / 0.80-1 almost perfect). Weighted kappa for ordinal scores; Krippendorff's alpha for multi-rater. The 6-step calibration loop (gold set + cadence + kappa + trend + threshold + recalibrate). The 90/10 production split with the ~59.8% production-AI-team statistic from Vadim 2026. When NOT to use LLM-as-judge: high-stakes / regulated / novel / adversarial / subjectively-contested. Path 06 trust stack assembly. Six references including the foundational Zheng et al. 2023 NeurIPS paper, FutureAGI's 2026 bias-mitigation taxonomy, Adaline's JudgeBiasBench finding (frontier models exceed 50% error on advanced bias tests), Dubois et al. 2024 length-controlled AlpacaEval, the Landis & Koch 1977 kappa thresholds.
  - `labs/20-drift-detection-and-calibration/README.md` (lab brief) — Two-halves framing with mermaid diagram. 10-outcome goal, prerequisites (the two new concept pages required), tools/versions (scipy + numpy + sklearn + matplotlib all already pinned; evidently as optional), 13-step structure split between Half A (Drift Detection, Steps 0-7) and Half B (Calibration, Steps 8-12) with Synthesis (Step 13). 8 gotchas. 8-item anti-scope. Cost/timing: ~$0 (all local computation, no API keys required).
  - `labs/20-drift-detection-and-calibration/lab.ipynb` — 34 cells (19 md / 15 code), output-stripped, end-to-end execution-verified. **Half A**: Step 0 setup → Step 1 baseline `Beta(8, 2)` distribution → Step 2 three drift scenarios (Beta(6,3) gradual, Beta(4,6) abrupt, Beta(20,5) shape-only with same mean as baseline) → Step 3 `ks_2samp` on each → Step 4 PSI hand-coded from first principles with the <0.1/0.25 threshold interpretation → Step 5 `wasserstein_distance` showing scale-aware magnitudes → Step 6 comparison table showing KS catches all three but Wasserstein underestimates Scenario C → Step 7 rolling-window detector on 1000-sample stream with mid-stream drift event, dual-plot of p-value (log scale) + KS statistic over time. **Half B**: Step 8 10-example human gold set with varied length and quality (intentionally small to keep the calibration loop visible) → Step 9 simulated naive LLM judge with verbosity bias (score weights length more than markers) → Step 10 baseline Cohen's kappa with Landis & Koch interpretation function → Step 11 measure-and-mitigate two biases (length bias analysis by quartile; length-controlled judge mitigation; kappa goes from 0.000 baseline to 1.000 after mitigation on the lab's synthetic data) → Step 12 12-week recalibration loop with injected judge-drift event at week 6, detected at week 9 (3-week detection lag). **Synthesis**: Step 13 — the four-layer Path 06 trust stack (instrumentation → online evaluation → drift detection → calibration) with table of what fails silently if any layer is skipped.
  - `quizzes/evaluation/drift-and-calibration.md` — 8 single-select questions in the YAML + `<details>` format. Three on drift mechanism (the three drift flavors; KS-test for shape changes; PSI for interpretable thresholds), three on calibration mechanism (the five named biases including a distractor; kappa interpretation; permutation averaging for position bias), two on decision boundary (the 90/10 split; the missing-calibration-layer failure mode where drift fires without actionable changes). Passing: 6/8.

- **Path 06 Module 4 — Online evaluation + tail-based sampling (Batch 27).** Third Path 06 lab. The production-runtime layer that sits on top of Modules 2 and 3: register evaluators against the live trace stream (platform-side via LangSmith Rules or code-side via SDK polling); sample intelligently at the Collector layer with the `tail_sampling` processor; close the production-to-fixture loop via annotation queues. Module 4 is the fourth of seven planned modules.
  - `concepts/evaluation/online-evaluator-registration.md` (~13 min) — The shift from offline (Lab 09/16 fixture-driven harnesses) to online (live trace stream). LangSmith Automations as the canonical mechanism: `(filter, sample_rate, action)` triples. Six action types in their canonical within-rule execution order: annotation queue → dataset → webhook → online evaluator → custom code evaluator → alert. The cross-rule polling gotcha: each rule runs on independent polling schedules, so a webhook in one rule may fire before an evaluator in another rule scores — dependencies must be expressed via filters. The Python SDK polling pattern as the code-side equivalent: `client.list_runs(project_name=..., execution_order=1)` + iterate + `client.create_feedback(run_id, key=..., score=...)`. When to use UI Rules vs SDK polling (a 5-row comparison table). Reference-free evaluators as the production reality — structural-property checks (citation_preservation, format_validity), LLM-as-judge with criteria-only prompts, heuristic confidence proxies. The closed loop in practice: 4-rule pattern that stages production → annotation → dataset → CI gating. LangSmith Engine (May 2026) as the AI layer on top — clusters failing traces, drafts evaluators, promotes failures into datasets automatically. Six references including LangChain docs on Rules and Evaluation, LangSmith Engine blog post, and Zheng et al. 2023 for LLM-as-judge biases.
  - `concepts/evaluation/tail-based-sampling.md` (~12 min) — Head-vs-tail distinction with code examples for each. Where tail sampling lives (OTel Collector, not the application). Six policy types from the `tailsamplingprocessor` README: `status_code`, `latency`, `numeric_attribute` (the agent-trace specifics — `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens > 5000`), `string_attribute`, `probabilistic`, `boolean_attribute`, `composite`. First-match-wins evaluation order. A representative production policy stack (errors / high-latency / high-token-usage / debug-users / 5% baseline) with concrete retention arithmetic showing ~7% retention at typical agent-trace distributions, ~14x storage cost reduction. The load-balancing constraint — all spans for a trace must reach the same Collector instance — and the two-tier topology with `loadbalancingexporter` that fixes it for multi-instance deployments. Memory budget arithmetic: `num_traces ≈ traces/sec × decision_wait × safety_margin`. When tail sampling earns its place vs LangSmith Rules: comparison table on layer, what you save, vendor scope, buffering required. The complementary pattern (use both). Five references including the official `tailsamplingprocessor` README, Grafana's applied guide, and oneuptime's two-tier-topology and policy-config deep-dives.
  - `labs/19-online-evaluation-and-sampling/README.md` (lab brief) — Two-halves framing with mermaid diagram. 10-outcome goal list, prerequisites, tools/versions (just `pyyaml` as a new dep), 11-step structure split between Half A (LangSmith Online Evaluation, Steps 0-6) and Half B (Tail-based Sampling, Steps 7-10) with synthesis (Step 11). 8 gotchas to watch for. 8-item anti-scope. Cost/timing (~$0.005 total — mostly free; one optional LLM-as-judge variant is the only cost source).

- **Path 06 Module 3 — OpenTelemetry portable layer (Batch 26).** Second Path 06 lab. The vendor-neutral counterpart to Module 2's LangSmith-native path.   - `concepts/evaluation/opentelemetry-genai-conventions.md` (~14 min) — The OpenTelemetry GenAI Special Interest Group, formed April 2024 to solve the pre-2024 fragmentation problem (`model` vs `llm.model` vs `openai.model` — same idea, three queries). The six layers of GenAI conventions: events, exceptions, metrics, model spans, agent spans, client spans. Core attributes that stabilized: `gen_ai.system`, `gen_ai.request.model`, `gen_ai.response.model`, sampling params, `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens`/`output_tokens`, `gen_ai.tool.name`/`call.id`, `gen_ai.agent.name`/`description`/`id`. The v1.37 transition replacing per-message events with three aggregated attributes (`gen_ai.system_instructions`, `gen_ai.input.messages`, `gen_ai.output.messages`) — and why (per-message events flooded multi-turn correlation). `OTEL_SEMCONV_STABILITY_OPT_IN=gen_ai_latest_experimental` env var pattern for the transition. Span-kind discipline (`CLIENT` for LLM calls is critical — without it APMs group LLM spans as "internal database operations"; `INTERNAL` for tools and agents). Auto-instrumentation via `OpenAIInstrumentor`, `opentelemetry-instrumentation-anthropic`, `opentelemetry-instrumentation-langchain`, and OpenLLMetry (Traceloop, Apache 2.0, 7,100+ stars, covering 8+ frameworks). Six references including the official OTel GenAI spec, the OTel blog kickoff post, and Greptime's May 2026 deep-dive on the v1.37 transition.
  - `concepts/evaluation/platform-fanout-and-portability.md` (~10 min) — The fanout pattern: one `TracerProvider`, multiple `SpanProcessors`, multiple exporters. Three configurations: dev (console + LangSmith), staging/CI (LangSmith + APM), production (OTel Collector-based with self-hosted backends). The `langsmith-collector-proxy` for GenAI-span filtering. Platform landscape with 6 agent-native platforms in a comparison table (LangSmith / Phoenix / Langfuse / Laminar / Braintrust / OpenAI Agents native) and 4 APM tools that ingest GenAI OTel but aren't agent-native (Datadog / New Relic / Honeycomb / Grafana Tempo). The lock-in cost reframed: the lock-in lives in the instrumentation (`@traceable` vs OTel manual spans), not the platform. Decision boundary for picking Lab 17 vs Lab 18 paths. A concrete five-stage migration story showing how a team navigates from LangSmith-native (Lab 17 pattern) to OTel-native (Lab 18 pattern) as corporate observability needs emerge. Six references.
  - `labs/18-opentelemetry-portable-tracing/README.md` (lab brief) — Sections: what you'll build (mermaid diagram), goal (9 outcomes), prerequisites, tools/versions table for the three new packages (`opentelemetry-sdk`, `opentelemetry-exporter-otlp`, `opentelemetry-instrumentation-openai`), 11-step structure (Step 7 Jaeger fanout marked optional), 8 gotchas to watch for (span kind being critical, OpenAIInstrumentor idempotence, `OTEL_SEMCONV_STABILITY_OPT_IN`, LangSmith OTLP endpoint paths, console output non-negotiability, Jaeger via OTLP not Thrift, auto+manual instrumentation cooperation, LangSmith ingesting both formats), 7 anti-scope deferrals, cost/timing (same ~$0.05-0.20 as Lab 17, 90-110 min).

  - `labs/14-langgraph-supervisor-bridge/solution/lab.ipynb` — 21 cells (12 md / 9 code), output-stripped. Canonical LangGraph supervisor-researcher-writer build. Provider-agnostic chat model via `init_chat_model` env switch. `SupervisorState(MessagesState)` TypedDict with `findings`, `citations`, `brief_status`, `final_answer`, `last_worker` fields. Researcher uses `create_agent` (LangGraph prebuilt) with `web_search` + `fetch_page` tools; writer is prompt-only `llm.invoke`. Supervisor is a manual node (NOT `create_supervisor()`, per upstream deprecation) using `@tool`-stub routing tools + `Command(goto=..., update=...)`. Graph compiled with `InMemorySaver` checkpointer. One end-to-end demonstration. ~40% reduction in pedagogical scaffolding vs parent's 33 cells.
  - `labs/14-langgraph-supervisor-bridge/solution/README.md` — Solution reference matching the Batch 14 template (parent link, concept-page pointers, quiz pointer, build-on pointer, "What this solution implements" with headline-path bullets + "Not in this solution" line, "Implementation choices" with five design decisions, "Common variations that also work" with four variations, "Running the solution" with wall-clock + cost). Five design decisions flagged: (1) manual supervisor via tool-calling not `create_supervisor()`; (2) state extends `MessagesState` not Pydantic (framework constraint); (3) routing tools are stubs intercepted at the node; (4) `recursion_limit=12` maps Lab 10's caps; (5) action-hash dedup lives at the tool layer.
  - `labs/15-langgraph-plan-execute-bridge/solution/lab.ipynb` — 25 cells (14 md / 11 code), output-stripped. Canonical LangGraph plan-and-execute build using `Send` for dynamic parallel dispatch. The strong framework value case: Lab 12's ~70-line manual `ThreadPoolExecutor` + `threading.Lock` collapses to ~10 lines via `[Send("executor", payload) for s in ready]`. `PlanStep` + `Plan` Pydantic `StrictModel` schemas, `validate_graph()` (Kahn's algorithm + 4 checks), `PlanState(TypedDict)` with `_merge_results` reducer on `completed`, planner-node with validation-then-retry, executor-node with `tc.name == step.tool` enforcement, dispatcher returning `list[Send]`, replanner returning `Command(goto="planner"|"synthesize")`, synthesizer with citation preservation. Plan-signature dedup omitted (Lab 12-specific stretch). ~22% size reduction vs parent's 32 cells.
  - `labs/15-langgraph-plan-execute-bridge/solution/README.md` — Same template structure as Lab 14's. Five design decisions flagged: (1) dispatcher transformation is the framework's payoff (~70 lines → ~10 lines); (2) `_merge_results` reducer is non-negotiable for parallel updates; (3) executor receives only its step + dependency outputs not the whole plan (same discipline as Lab 12); (4) validation-then-retry stays in the planner node; (5) plan-signature dedup omitted in canonical solution (mentioned as common variation).
  - `labs/16-multi-agent-evaluation-from-scratch/solution/lab.ipynb` — 19 cells (12 md / 7 code), output-stripped. Canonical evaluation harness for multi-agent trajectories. Same algorithm as the parent — verified by re-execution: aggregate handoff_success=0.892, routing_accuracy=0.967, citation_preservation=0.811, groundedness=0.411 (identical to parent). Pydantic `StrictModel` for `TraceStep` + `Trace`. Seven metrics as standalone pure functions: `handoff_success_rate`, `routing_accuracy` (LCS-based), `plan_validity`, `plan_coverage`, `replan_rate` (set-level), `citation_preservation` (URL canonicalization, dict return with preservation/hallucinated/dropped), `groundedness` (rule-based lexical overlap, conservative-by-design). `run_harness(trace_set) → DataFrame`. Aggregate-vs-slice + per-agent breakdown. ~41% size reduction vs parent's 32 cells.
  - `labs/16-multi-agent-evaluation-from-scratch/solution/README.md` — Same template structure. Four design decisions flagged: (1) each metric is a pure function (no globals, testable in isolation, ready for `harness.py` extraction); (2) URL canonicalization for citation comparison (strip trailing slash, fragment, tracking params); (3) `citation_preservation` returns a dict not a scalar (three subscores avoid Schroedinger's metric on dropped-vs-hallucinated); (4) rule-based groundedness is conservative-by-design (false negatives cheap, false positives expensive).
  - `.lycheeignore` — Modified. Removes the three forward-references for Lab 14/15/16 solution directories now that they're authored. Updates the solutions-authored note to track Batch 23. No new entries.

- **Path 03 Module 6 — Multi-agent evaluation, Lab 16 (multi-agent evaluation harness from scratch).** Closes Path 03 v1's curriculum. Extends Lab 09's RAG-evaluation harness pattern (hand-curated fixtures + rule-based tier + LLM-as-judge tier + category slicing) for multi-agent trajectories. Same discipline, different unit of analysis — a recorded trajectory instead of a single query/answer pair.
  - `concepts/multi-agent/multi-agent-evaluation.md` — ~13 min. The framing. Trajectory metrics (the path) vs outcome metrics (the answer); why neither alone is sufficient. The replay model (deterministic, cheap, diagnostic, CI-friendly) and its trade-off (can't catch live-state behavior). The trace fixture as the eval contract. Per-agent vs end-to-end evaluation. What this misses (long-running, adversarial, multi-turn, agent-as-judge calibration, production tooling). References McKinsey QuantumBlack (Jan 2026), Vertex AI's `trajectory_exact_match` / `trajectory_precision` / `trajectory_recall` formalization, LangChain `agentevals`, Zheng et al. 2023 (LLM-as-judge biases).
  - `concepts/multi-agent/trajectory-level-metrics.md` — ~12 min. The implementation companion. Trace shape (`TraceStep` + `Trace` Pydantic models). Five trajectory metrics — `handoff_success_rate`, `routing_accuracy`, `plan_validity`, `plan_coverage`, `replan_rate` — and two outcome metrics — `citation_preservation`, `groundedness` — each with Python signature and "what it reveals / what it hides" lines. Citation preservation includes URL canonicalization (strip trailing slashes, fragments, tracking params) and four failure-mode subtypes (drop / duplicate / hallucinated / renumbered). Category-slicing discipline with the healthy-system-signature table. Per-agent vs end-to-end framing. Headline metric pattern by system type.
  - `labs/16-multi-agent-evaluation-from-scratch/lab.ipynb` — 32 cells (21 md / 11 code), output-stripped. Step 0 setup with provider-agnostic config. Step 1 pydantic `StrictModel` for `TraceStep` + `Trace`; load and validate `trace_set.jsonl`. Steps 2-6 implement the seven metrics from scratch (LCS-based routing accuracy; binary plan validity reading the planner's `plan_valid` flag; plan coverage as `successful_executors / plan_step_count`; set-level replan rate; citation preservation with URL canonicalization and `{preservation, hallucinated, dropped}` subscores; rule-based groundedness via lexical overlap on content words ≥4 chars). Step 7 runs the full harness, produces the comparison table, demonstrates aggregate-vs-slice. Step 8 per-agent breakdown via groupby on the trajectory. Step 9 (optional) LLM-as-judge plan-validity variant using provider-agnostic client. Step 10 synthesis closing Path 03 v1.

- **Path 03 Module 5 — Framework bridge, Labs 14+15 (LangGraph supervisor + plan-and-execute bridges).** Two framework-bridge labs that rebuild Lab 10 (supervisor-worker) and Lab 12 (plan-and-execute) in LangGraph and provide line-by-line comparisons against the from-scratch baselines. The pedagogical payoff of Path 03's "from-scratch first" approach: with working code on both sides, framework-adoption trade-offs become concrete rather than abstract.
  - `concepts/multi-agent/langgraph-multi-agent.md` — ~15 min. Maps LangGraph's five multi-agent primitives onto from-scratch concepts: `StateGraph` + `TypedDict` state ↔ supervisor's `messages` list + side-state; `Command(goto=..., update=..., graph=...)` ↔ tool-call dispatch + structured-error envelope; `Send(node, state)` ↔ manual `ThreadPoolExecutor`; sub-graphs ↔ worker functions; checkpointer ↔ (no from-scratch analog). Each primitive carries a "what you gain / what you trade away" comparison. The three multi-agent topologies LangGraph names (supervisor, swarm, hierarchical). What carries over unchanged: prompts, worker contracts, citation discipline.
  - `concepts/multi-agent/when-frameworks-earn-complexity.md` — ~10 min. The boundary discussion. Five things from-scratch pays for (context engineering control, zero upgrade risk, debugging transparency, cost/latency reasoning, pattern portability); five things framework pays for (checkpointer, `Send`, observability hooks, reducer-based merge, HITL primitives); a decision table for when each fits. References the upstream `langgraph-supervisor` deprecation note as evidence that high-level multi-agent helpers age poorly because the underlying patterns evolve faster than the helpers.
  - `labs/14-langgraph-supervisor-bridge/lab.ipynb` — 33 cells (21 md / 12 code). Rebuilds Lab 10's supervisor-researcher-writer in LangGraph using the manual supervisor-via-tools pattern (per LangChain's current recommendation; NOT `create_supervisor()` which is deprecated for new code). Researcher uses `create_agent` with `web_search` + `fetch_page` tools (same as Lab 10). Supervisor returns `Command(goto="researcher" | "writer" | "__end__", update={...})`. Step caps mapped onto `recursion_limit=12`. Demonstrates `InMemorySaver` for crash-resume, `graph.stream()` for observable execution, and the `Command(goto=..., graph=Command.PARENT)` primitive as the building block for swarm topology. Closes with a line-by-line comparison table showing framework wins on plumbing (chat client ~50 lines, researcher loop ~70 lines, supervisor dispatch ~30 lines) and stays unchanged on prompts and worker contracts.
  - `labs/15-langgraph-plan-execute-bridge/lab.ipynb` — 32 cells (20 md / 12 code). Rebuilds Lab 12's plan-and-execute in LangGraph using the `Send` primitive — the strong framework value case. `Plan`/`PlanStep` Pydantic schemas, `validate_graph` (Kahn's algorithm + 4 checks), planner prompt (same five rules as Lab 12), executor with anti-improvement enforcement (`tc.name == step.tool`), synthesizer — all carry over unchanged from Lab 12. The dispatcher reduces from ~70 lines of manual `ThreadPoolExecutor` + `threading.Lock` + completion tracking to ~10 lines of `return [Send("executor", payload) for s in ready]`. The `completed` state field uses a custom reducer (`_merge_results`) to handle parallel updates from `Send`-dispatched executors. Replanner becomes a graph edge via `Command(goto="planner", update={...})`. Plan-signature dedup carries over from Lab 12 (lives in a state field rather than a closure variable). Closes with a line-by-line comparison table showing the dispatcher transformation as the dominant change.
  - `labs/14-langgraph-supervisor-bridge/README.md` — 130-160 min lab, intermediate. Lists prerequisites (Lab 10 mandatory, Lab 05 mandatory for LangGraph familiarity), structure overview, line-by-line comparison table (chat client, researcher worker, writer worker, supervisor system prompt, supervisor loop, worker schemas, crash-resume, streaming), five practical issues to watch for, full anti-scope section.
  - `labs/15-langgraph-plan-execute-bridge/README.md` — 130-160 min lab, intermediate. Same structure as Lab 14 README. Line-by-line comparison table highlighting that planner prompt, validation logic, executor agent, synthesizer prompt all carry over unchanged from Lab 12; the dispatcher transformation is the only major code change. Five practical issues (Send payload self-containment, reducer requirement, implicit concurrency cap, replanner-as-graph-edge debugging cost, parallel-executor state debugging).
  - `quizzes/multi-agent/framework-bridge.md` — 8 single-select questions on `Command` semantics, `Send` vs `ThreadPoolExecutor`, the `langgraph-supervisor` deprecation reasoning, what the checkpointer adds, supervisor vs swarm trade-offs, reducer requirements on parallel-update state fields, when migration isn't worth it, and what the framework comparison demonstrates. Standard format: YAML front-matter with `questions:` list, then markdown body with `<details>` reveals.
  - `diagrams/langgraph-supervisor-flow.mmd` — top-down `flowchart TD` of Lab 14's supervisor flow. Subgraph blocks grouping: User request, Supervisor, Workers, State updates, Final response. Short labels for GitHub rendering.
  - `diagrams/langgraph-send-dispatch-flow.mmd` — top-down `flowchart TD` of Lab 15's `Send` dispatch flow. Subgraph blocks grouping: User request, Planner, Dispatcher (the `Send` primitive), Parallel executors, State merge via reducer, Replanner, Synthesizer, Final response. Split from Lab 14's diagram (two related diagrams rather than one wide chain) to keep GitHub rendering readable.

- **Solutions batch — reference implementations for Labs 10/11/12/13.** Polished reference solutions for Path 03's four labs land together. Same pattern as batch 14 for Labs 01-09. Each solution targets ~40-60% of its parent's cell count: leaner notebook (the parent teaches; the solution calibrates), substantive README covering implementation choices, common variations, and bugs that pass syntax but fail eval.
  - `labs/10-supervisor-worker-from-scratch/solution/lab.ipynb` — 17 cells (10 md / 7 code). Provider-agnostic `chat_with_tools`, web tools (web_search + fetch_page from Lab 03), researcher worker (`WORKER_MAX_STEPS=8`), writer worker, supervisor with `CallResearcherArgs` + `CallWriterArgs` StrictModel schemas, `SUPERVISOR_MAX_STEPS=6`, action-hash dedup at supervisor level. Five design decisions worth flagging in the README: Pydantic StrictModel for worker args (rejects LLM-invented fields); action-hash dedup at supervisor level (not inside workers); worker-as-function not class (stateless); step caps escalate via structured `{status: "step_cap"}` envelopes (not exceptions); supervisor system prompt names worker contract explicitly. Headline path only — no dedup demo, no step-cap envelope walkthrough, no unknown-worker fallback demo, no critic preview.
  - `labs/11-generator-critic-from-scratch/solution/lab.ipynb` — 17 cells (10 md / 7 code). All Lab 10 machinery reused unchanged; new critic worker with five `CRITIC_ISSUE_KINDS = [unsupported_claim, missing_citation, dropped_citation, unclear_prose, format_violation]`; updated supervisor (`SUPERVISOR_MAX_STEPS=10`, raised from Lab 10's 6) with `call_writer_critic_loop` as a single composed tool encapsulating writer+critic+refinement; `MAX_REFINEMENT_CYCLES=3` cap fires out-of-sample with `ok_with_unresolved_issues` (NOT forced `ok`). Six design decisions in the README: enumerated CRITIC_ISSUE_KINDS (not free-text); issues capped at 3 per call; critic defaults to OK on borderline (anti-sycophancy mirror); critic gets brief + draft (not just draft); bounded refinement with structured revision context (not "try again, be better"); cap fires out-of-sample. Headline path only — no sycophancy diagnostic, no failure-mode walkthrough, no self-critique stretch.
  - `labs/12-plan-and-execute-from-scratch/solution/lab.ipynb` — 21 cells (12 md / 9 code). All Lab 10 machinery; `PlanStep`/`Plan` StrictModel schemas with `validate_graph()` running Kahn's algorithm + 4 other checks (duplicate IDs, unknown tools, unknown dependencies, parallel-group integrity) in one pass; planner agent with retry loop emitting validation errors back to the planner; executor agent with anti-improvement validation (`tc.name == step.tool` enforced after LLM emits tool call); pure-Python `dispatch_plan` with `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=3)` and `threading.Lock` on shared state; `plan_and_execute` with `_plan_signature` dedup (hashes structural content only, NOT descriptions) escalating identical-plan replans to `partial_after_cap`; synthesizer. Constants: `MAX_PLAN_STEPS=8`, `MAX_PARALLEL_EXECUTORS=3`, `EXECUTOR_MAX_STEPS=4`, `MAX_REPLANS=2`, `SUPERVISOR_MAX_STEPS=12`. Six design decisions in the README: dispatcher is pure Python not LLM-driven; plan signature uses structural content only; `validate_graph` runs five checks in one pass; executor stateless and sees only `(step, dep_outputs)`; anti-improvement enforced structurally not just in prompt; thread-safety via explicit `threading.Lock`. Headline path only — no failure-mode walkthrough, no ReAct comparison stretch.
  - `labs/13-multi-agent-rag-from-scratch/solution/lab.ipynb` — 19 cells (11 md / 8 code). The integrative solution. All Lab 10 machinery; inline chunker matching Lab 06 (`TARGET_TOKENS=160`, `OVERLAP_TOKENS=32` pinned for Lab 08 cache compatibility); full Lab 07 v2 retrieval pipeline inline (dense MiniLM + BM25 + RRF k=60 + cross-encoder rerank); v3 auto-detect when Lab 08's `context_cache.json` is available with 90% chunk-ID coverage check (silent parameter-drift catch); `retriever_agent` with `MIN_RERANK_SCORE=-2.0`; synthesizer with chunk_id citation discipline; supervisor with `CallRetrieverArgs` + `CallSynthesizerArgs` schemas, corpus description + four retrieval-decision rules in system prompt, `SUPERVISOR_MAX_STEPS=6`. Six design decisions in the README: retriever is function wrapper not class (module-level closure for state); supervisor never sees retrieval internals (no BM25/dense/rerank scores in routing reasoning); chunks pass through supervisor VERBATIM (structural citation drift mitigation); synthesizer cites by chunk_id not auto-numbered [1]/[2]; v2/v3 auto-detect with 90% coverage threshold; `MIN_RERANK_SCORE = -2.0` empirical floor. Headline path only — no retrieve/skip diagnostic, no failure-mode walkthrough, no Lab 11 critic-on-synthesis stretch, no Lab 12 planner-driven parallel-retrievals stretch.

- **Path 03 Module 4 — Multi-agent RAG, Lab 13 (multi-agent RAG from scratch).** The integrative module. Composes Path 02's retrieval pipeline (Labs 06-08) with Path 03's coordination patterns (Labs 10-12) into a single multi-agent RAG system.
  - `concepts/multi-agent/multi-agent-rag.md` — ~10 min concept page. The framing of the pattern family. What changes from single-agent RAG (Lab 06-08): retrieval becomes a coordinated concern, not a single always-on tool call. The three architectural patterns with tradeoffs — **retriever-as-worker** (the headline; supervisor decides when to retrieve), **planner-driven research** (Lab 12 composition; planner emits parallel retrievals for compound queries), **critic-on-retrieval** (Lab 11 composition; critic reviews chunks before synthesis or reviews synthesis for groundedness). When multi-agent RAG earns its place over single-agent RAG (deciding-to-retrieve is non-trivial, multiple retrievals must be composed, precision justifies a critic). When single-agent RAG is enough (always-retrieve workloads, one-retrieval-per-query workloads, retrieval-precision-already-sufficient workloads). The four multi-agent-RAG-specific failure modes — **citation drift** (citations lost in retriever → supervisor → synthesizer handoff), **retrieval skip** (supervisor decides not to retrieve when it should have), **retrieval over-call** (supervisor retrieves for stable-knowledge queries), **chunk drift** (synthesizer paraphrases beyond what chunks support). When self-RAG (Asai 2023, training-time intervention) and CRAG (Yan 2024, retrieval-evaluator-with-fallback) are the right pattern instead.
  - `concepts/multi-agent/retriever-as-worker.md` — ~10 min concept page. The specific pattern Lab 13 implements. The shape: supervisor with corpus description + four retrieval-decision rules, retriever-worker wrapping Lab 06-08's pipeline as a single function returning `{status, chunks: [{id, text, source, title, score}], query, pipeline}` envelope, synthesizer with chunk_id citation discipline. **The four retrieval-decision rules** with concrete failure-mode mappings: (1) retrieve when corpus-grounded; (2) don't retrieve for stable-knowledge questions; (3) one retrieval per distinct factual question (action-hash dedup enforces); (4) pass retrieved chunks directly to synthesizer without paraphrase. **Citation preservation discipline** as a 5-step chain of custody: retriever assigns stable `id`s → retriever-worker returns envelope → supervisor passes envelope verbatim → synthesizer cites by `[chunk_id]` → final answer renders citation list. Choosing the retrieval pipeline (v2 vs v3 auto-detect based on whether Lab 08's `context_cache.json` is available, with a 90% chunk-ID coverage check to catch parameter drift). Composing with Lab 11's critic on synthesis (the standard placement, not critic-on-chunks). Composing with Lab 12's planner for compound queries (retriever as one of the planner's available tools).

- **Path 03 Module 3 — Plan-and-execute, Lab 12 (planner-executor from scratch).**
  - `concepts/multi-agent/plan-and-execute.md` — ~10 min concept page. The framing of the pattern family. What changes from supervisor-worker (Lab 10) and generator-critic (Lab 11): the planner emits the *whole trajectory* upfront as a structured artifact; the supervisor becomes a dependency-resolving dispatcher; parallel execution earns its place legitimately. When plan-and-execute beats the alternatives: tasks with clear upfront decomposition, tasks where steps are independent enough to parallelize, tasks where the plan itself is the artifact. When it doesn't: single-step tasks, fully-serial tasks, exploratory tasks where the plan can't be known upfront (ReAct territory), tasks where replanning cost approaches original planning cost. Plan-first vs interleaved planning; the parallelism trade-off (wall-clock optimization, not cost or quality). The four plan-and-execute-specific failure modes — **plan brittleness** (planner guessed structure of tool results that didn't hold; role-based descriptions are the fix), **execution drift** (executor "improves" beyond what the plan specified; anti-improvement prompt is the fix), **replanning thrash** (every failure triggers a re-plan; `MAX_REPLANS = 2` cap + structural plan-signature dedup is the fix), **plan-execution gap** (planner uses tools the executor doesn't have; tool-registry-in-planner-prompt is the fix).
  - `concepts/multi-agent/planner-executor-pattern.md` — ~10 min concept page. The specific pattern Lab 12 implements. The shape: planner → Plan artifact → supervisor + dispatcher → executor pool → synthesizer, with bounded replanning. Plan representation as `PlanStep` + `Plan` Pydantic schemas with `id`, `description`, `tool`, `args`, `depends_on`, `parallel_group` fields; `StrictModel(extra="forbid")` enforces no extra fields. **The five planner-prompt rules**: (1) atomic steps; (2) explicit dependencies; (3) unbiased parallel groups; (4) self-contained descriptions; (5) bounded plans. Executor pool concurrency (`ThreadPoolExecutor`, `MAX_PARALLEL_EXECUTORS = 3` — IO-bound LLM calls; threads are fine; no asyncio complexity). Replanning policy (`MAX_REPLANS = 2` with identical-plan dedup). Four-cap composition: `MAX_PLAN_STEPS = 8`, `MAX_PARALLEL_EXECUTORS = 3`, `EXECUTOR_MAX_STEPS = 4`, `MAX_REPLANS = 2`, `SUPERVISOR_MAX_STEPS = 12`. "Composing with Lab 10 and Lab 11" section: reuses chat client, web tools, action-hash dedup, `StrictModel` verbatim; the dispatcher is pure Python (not LLM-driven) because dependency resolution is mechanical.

New "Module 3 — Plan-and-execute (batch 17)" section with two concept pages + Lab 12 + quiz. "What comes next" shifted (Modules 4-6: multi-agent RAG, framework bridge, multi-agent evaluation). References list extended with Wang 2023 (Plan-and-Solve), Yao 2023 (ReAct), Xu 2024 (AgentBench).

Four agents: planner (emits JSON-validated Plan), executor (one step at a time with anti-improvement framing), supervisor (Python dispatcher with `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=3)`), synthesizer (final answer composer). Explicit anti-scope: no CrewAI / AutoGen / LangGraph multi-agent helpers, no Tree-of-Thoughts / MCTS, no async/asyncio orchestration, no distributed execution / persistent plan state, no multi-agent RAG (future batch), no MCP/A2A (Path 04), no production observability (Path 06). What-to-watch-for list (5 items): JSON validation retry loops, parallel-group unbiasedy (validator catches violations), thread-safety via `threading.Lock`, cost (~8-15 calls per run; replanning thrash can double this), wall-clock vs sequential timing.
  - `labs/12-plan-and-execute-from-scratch/lab.ipynb` — 40-cell notebook (25 md / 15 code), output-stripped. Step 0 setup. Step 1 compact recap of Lab 10/11 machinery (chat client, web tools, action-hash, `StrictModel`, `_strip_code_fences`). Step 2 `PlanStep` / `Plan` Pydantic schemas with `validate_graph()` method that detects cycles (Kahn's algorithm), duplicate IDs, unknown tools, parallel-group violations, and unknown dependency references. Step 3 the planner agent — `_format_tool_registry` injects the executor's tool registry into the system prompt (closing the plan-execution gap structurally); validation-then-retry loop catches JSON parse errors, Pydantic schema errors, graph errors with specific feedback to the planner. Step 4 the executor agent — `EXECUTOR_TOOL_REGISTRY` exposed at the executor level; tight anti-improvement system prompt; `tc.name == step.tool` validation that returns `wrong_tool` envelope on deviation; supports `cannot_execute` structured signal. Step 5 the dispatcher — `_ready_steps()`, `_group_parallel()`, `dispatch_plan()` with `ThreadPoolExecutor`, `threading.Lock` on shared state, action-hash dedup at the pool level. Step 6 the replanning hook — `_plan_signature()` computes structural hash (steps, tools, args, deps, parallel groups — *not* descriptions); `plan_and_execute()` with `MAX_REPLANS = 2` and identical-plan escalation. Step 7 the synthesizer — citation preservation, unbiased surfacing of failed steps. Step 8 end-to-end run with verbose trace showing planning → parallel dispatch → synthesis. Step 9 four-failure-mode walkthrough with concrete code demonstrations: plan brittleness (role-based descriptions), execution drift (tool-name validation), replanning thrash (plan-signature dedup with two-plan example showing structural vs prose differences), plan-execution gap (validator rejecting unknown tools). Step 10 (stretch) plan-and-execute vs ReAct framing (which optimizes the median run, which optimizes the worst-case run).

All anchor links verified via github-slugger emulation against both concept pages.

- **Path 03 Module 2 — Agent debate and critics, Lab 11 (generator-critic from scratch).**
  - `concepts/multi-agent/agent-debate-and-critics.md` — ~10 min concept page. The general framing of iterative-refinement-via-critique. The pattern family: self-critique → generator-critic → multi-agent debate. When critique earns its place (high-stakes prose with citations, code that must compile, factual answers where wrong is worse than slow, anything with a clear eval rubric) and when it doesn't (stable factual recall, speed-critical workloads, no clear quality criterion). The four debate-specific failure modes — **sycophancy** (critic agrees regardless; Sharma et al. 2023 root cause), **infinite agreement** (stable mediocre state, critic and generator both happy), **runaway disagreement** (cap matters), **critique drift** (standards shift across rounds; stateless critic is the fix). Self-critique vs separate-critic trade. Explicit "what this pattern is not" section (Tree-of-Thoughts is parallel search, self-consistency is majority voting, Constitutional AI is training-time critique).
  - `concepts/multi-agent/generator-critic-pattern.md` — ~10 min concept page. The specific pattern Lab 11 implements. The shape: supervisor → generator → critic → if-approve-finalize-else-refine, bounded by `MAX_REFINEMENT_CYCLES = 3`. The critic's structured envelope: `{status: "ok"}` or `{status: "needs_revision", issues: [{kind, detail}]}` with enumerated kinds (`unsupported_claim`, `missing_citation`, `dropped_citation`, `unclear_prose`, `format_violation`). **The four critic-prompt-design rules** with concrete bad/good examples: (1) anchor to a checklist not a vibe; (2) make "ok" the default for borderline cases; (3) require specific evidence in each issue; (4) bound the issue list to ≤3. Sycophancy detection (the obvious-bad-draft test) and four mitigations (different temperature, adversarial framing, rubric anchors, different model). "Composing with Lab 10" section: the supervisor is unchanged in shape; the delta is one new tool, an updated prompt, and a step-cap bump from 6 to 10.

- **Path 03 v1 opening — Multi-Agent Systems (Module 1: foundations + supervisor-worker, Lab 10).**
  - `learning-paths/03-multi-agent-systems/README.md` — Path 03 entry README. Modeled on Path 02 v1's shape. Five numbered items across Module 1: 3 concept pages + Lab 10 + quiz. Time estimate 6-9 hours for this opening; path grows over subsequent batches. Explicit anti-scope: no frameworks (CrewAI/AutoGen/LangGraph multi-agent) in the headline lab, no agent debate or plan-and-execute (later modules), no MCP/A2A coverage (Path 04), no multi-agent RAG (later Path 03 batch), no production observability (Path 06). References list anchored to: Wang et al. 2023 survey, Wu et al. 2023 (AutoGen architecture paper, not the framework), Hong et al. 2023 (MetaGPT), Qian et al. 2023 (ChatDev), Park et al. 2023 (Generative Agents), Anthropic 2024 ("Building effective agents"), Wang et al. 2024 (multi-agent collaboration mechanisms survey).
  - `concepts/multi-agent/what-is-a-multi-agent-system.md` — ~10 min concept page. The unbiased framing of when multi-agent earns its complexity and when it doesn't. Three legitimate motivations (specialization, parallelism, debuggability) and four common mis-motivations ("more agents = better", "multi-agent = more autonomous", "multi-agent = production-grade", "multi-agent = parallel"). Quantitative framing of coordination cost (~2x LLM-call multiplier per handoff). Concrete patterns where multi-agent pays off (retrieval/synthesis split, planner/executor split, generator/critic split, domain specialization). Explicit "what multi-agent doesn't fix" list (weak tools, weak prompts, lack of RAG, hallucination, lack of evaluation).
  - `concepts/multi-agent/supervisor-worker-pattern.md` — ~10 min concept page. The pattern Lab 10 implements. Three properties (supervisor is a Lab 01 loop, supervisor's tools are calls to workers, workers don't talk to each other). Four reasons the pattern dominates production (clear control flow, obvious place for cross-cutting concerns, independently replaceable workers, failure-mode locality). Six failure modes and their mitigations (supervisor never escalates → step cap; same-args repeats → action-hash dedup; wrong worker chosen → Lab 02 negative-guidance descriptions; worker hallucinates capability → tight worker prompts + supervisor reads errors; handoff envelope mangles → structured payloads not free text; endless ping-pong → smaller step cap + dedup). When supervisor-worker isn't enough (highly parallel → plan-and-execute; iterative refinement → debate; symmetric peers → swarm).
  - `concepts/multi-agent/handoffs-and-shared-state.md` — ~9 min concept page. The two architectures (message-passing vs shared-state) with the trade laid out: message-passing wins on simplicity, debuggability, no race conditions, lower coupling; shared-state wins on efficiency at scale, parallel execution, long-running persistence; shared-state loses on race conditions, mutation-order semantics, coupling, debug surface. Heuristic: default to message-passing; reach for shared-state when message-passing genuinely creaks. The three handoff-hygiene rules: (1) handoffs carry structured payloads, not free text; (2) the supervisor decides the next step, not the worker; (3) every handoff is logged at the supervisor. Five most common multi-agent bugs in rough order of frequency.
  - `concepts/multi-agent/README.md` — section index. Lists the 3 current pages with read-times; forward-references planned future pages (agent-debate, plan-and-execute, multi-agent-evaluation) for IA legibility; cross-links to Path 03 README, Lab 10, and the adjacent `concepts/agents/`, `concepts/tools/`, `concepts/evaluation/` sections.
  - `labs/10-supervisor-worker-from-scratch/README.md` — lab brief (~100-130 min, intermediate). Goal: build a 3-agent system (supervisor + researcher + writer) using only the Path 01 agent-loop machinery. **No new dependencies** — the researcher reuses Lab 03's `web_search` + `fetch_page` verbatim; the writer is prompt-only; the supervisor's "tools" are Python functions wrapping the worker loops, dispatched through the same Lab 02 `chat_with_tools` contract. Step cap composition (supervisor: 6 / workers: 8) with structured-error escalation. Anti-scope: no frameworks, no parallel execution, no shared state, no multi-agent RAG, no production observability, no agent debate / plan-and-execute / swarm, no MCP / A2A. Common-gotchas list (citation drops across handoffs, supervisor over-calling researcher, writer scope creep, step-cap interaction across levels, wrong worker chosen).
  - `labs/10-supervisor-worker-from-scratch/lab.ipynb` — 33-cell notebook (22 md / 11 code). Step 0 setup. Step 1 researcher worker reusing Lab 03's `web_search`/`fetch_page`/citation tracking, wrapped as a function returning `{status, findings, citations}`. Step 2 writer worker with prompt-only loop, structured `{status, answer}` envelope, explicit citation-preservation instruction. Step 3 supervisor with Pydantic `StrictModel` worker schemas (Lab 02 pattern), negative-guidance descriptions, `SUPERVISOR_MAX_STEPS = 6` smaller than `WORKER_MAX_STEPS = 8`, supervisor system prompt taking explicit responsibility for routing and citation preservation. Step 4 end-to-end run on a real task with verbose trace. Step 5 failure-mode walkthroughs (action-hash dedup demonstration, worker step-cap envelope, unknown-worker dispatcher error). Step 6 (stretch) critic worker sketch using the same pattern. Notebook outputs stripped; sample-output markdown cells throughout. ~250 lines of Python total across the agent definitions.
  - `quizzes/multi-agent/multi-agent-fundamentals.md` — 8 single-select questions: (1) the "splitting feels more production-grade" mis-motivation; (2) coordination-cost ~2x estimate; (3) negative-guidance descriptions as the highest-leverage routing fix; (4) the canonical citation-loss bug and the structured-payload + prompt fix; (5) step-cap interaction across levels via structured-error escalation; (6) handoff-count as a routing-decisiveness signal not an architectural one; (7) when shared-state actually earns its complexity; (8) the prompt-level fix for repeated-near-duplicate worker calls. All anchors verified against actual heading slugs in the 3 concept pages. Same YAML front-matter + `<details>` reveals + trailing-2-space-hard-break format as all prior quizzes.
  - `diagrams/supervisor-worker-flow.mmd` — Mermaid flowchart for Lab 10's trajectory. User → supervisor → researcher → supervisor → writer → final, with subgraph nodes per agent showing each agent has its own internal loop. Failure-mode edges (dotted) show worker step-cap and needs-more-research escalation paths. 4-class-styled diagram (user / supervisor / worker / terminal) matching the diagrammatic vocabulary established in batches 11/12/13/14 (`rag-trajectory.mmd`, `research-agent-trajectory.mmd`, `retrieval-pipeline.mmd`).

- **Solutions batch — reference implementations for Labs 01-09.**
  - `labs/01-first-agent-from-scratch/solution/lab.ipynb` + `solution/README.md` — 16-cell consolidation of the agent loop with `_action_hash` dedup + step cap + structured-tool-error envelope; skips the lab's "build naïve first, then fix" walkthrough. Same provider-agnostic chat client as the lab; same Pydantic-validated tool registry; same `MAX_STEPS=8`. README documents the five implementation choices worth flagging (small dataclass over Pydantic at the boundary, Anthropic-branch text composition via `"".join(...)`, `json.dumps(sort_keys=True)` for signature stability, `>= 2` duplicate threshold calibration, structured-error envelope uniformity).
  - `labs/02-tool-design-and-selection/solution/lab.ipynb` + `solution/README.md` — 21-cell consolidation shipping only `tools_v1` (the working design). Six-tool customer-support toolset with `StrictModel(extra="forbid")`, `OrderStatus = Literal[...]` enum, `confirmation_required` gate as the headline pattern, negative-guidance descriptions ("Do NOT use this for X — use Y") on three of six tools. Skips the lab's v0 failure walk; the v1 design is the answer.
  - `labs/03-multi-step-research-agent/solution/lab.ipynb` + `solution/README.md` — 17-cell consolidation of the web-research agent. `web_search` + `fetch_page` with structured-error envelope on every documented failure mode (rate limit, timeout, http_4xx, blocked, paywall via body-marker heuristics, parse, too_long). Citation tracking by the loop (only on `status in ("ok", "too_long")` `fetch_page` results), never by the LLM. Tool results capped at 4000 chars before context append. Smooths the lab's mixed `msg["content"]` / `msg.content` access to consistent dataclass attribute access.
  - `labs/05-langgraph-rewrite/solution/lab.ipynb` + `solution/README.md` — 23-cell consolidation using `langchain.agents.create_agent` as the headline (NOT the deprecated `langgraph.prebuilt.create_react_agent` per the verified `tools/langgraph/snapshot-v1.0.md`). Five-line `create_agent` construction; `.get_graph().draw_ascii()` confirms the same topology the explicit `StateGraph` build produces. Preserves the checkpointer + `thread_id` durable-state pattern and the `interrupt()`-based human-in-the-loop pattern with a destructive-action `delete_customer` tool (`Command(resume=True/False)` flow).
  - `labs/06-agentic-rag-from-scratch/solution/lab.ipynb` + `solution/README.md` — 19-cell consolidation of the agentic RAG stack. `TARGET_TOKENS=160` + `OVERLAP_TOKENS=32` chunker config **pinned** to match the parent lab byte-for-byte (Labs 07-09 depend on the chunk IDs). `MIN_SIMILARITY=0.30` floor against MiniLM cosines for off-corpus query detection. `search_corpus` + `read_chunk` tools with same `status: ok/empty/error` envelope as Lab 03. Citations tracked structurally by the loop on successful `read_chunk`. Corpus path is `../corpus/` (solution lives one level deeper).
  - `labs/07-retrieval-strategies-and-reranking/solution/lab.ipynb` + `solution/README.md` — 25-cell consolidation of the four-stage retrieval pipeline as `search_corpus_v2`: dense (`all-MiniLM-L6-v2`) + BM25 (`rank_bm25.BM25Okapi`) → RRF (`k=60`) → cross-encoder rerank (`ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2`, candidate_k=30 → top_k=5). `MIN_SIMILARITY=0.0` against cross-encoder *logits* (not cosines — different scale from Lab 06; flagged explicitly). MMR exposed as `use_mmr=False` default. Same tool contract as Lab 06 so the agent loop is unchanged. `retrieval_signals` field carries per-retriever scores for debuggability.
  - `labs/08-contextual-retrieval-and-query-rewriting/solution/lab.ipynb` + `solution/README.md` — 26-cell consolidation of Lab 07's pipeline composed with contextual augmentation and three query-rewriting modes (`hyde`, `multi`, `decompose`) as `search_corpus_v3`. Anthropic's verbatim `CONTEXT_PROMPT` reproduced exactly. Cache-or-generate pattern reads/writes `../context_cache.json` (one level deeper than the lab). Rerank uses the *original* query, not the rewrites — the rewrites help retrieval, the rerank should score against what the user actually asked. Results return *original* chunk text — augmentation was indexing-only; the agent reads the chunk.
  - `labs/09-evaluating-agentic-rag/solution/lab.ipynb` + `solution/README.md` — 33-cell from-scratch eval harness. Loads `../eval_set.jsonl` (30 hand-curated queries across 5 categories). 4 retrieval pipelines as same-shape callables (`(query, top_k) → list[chunk_id]`): `dense_baseline`, `hybrid_rrf`, `hybrid_rerank`, `contextual_rerank` (auto-skipped if `../context_cache.json` missing). 4 retrieval metrics as pure functions (`hits@k`, `recall@k`, `MRR`, `rank_of_expected_doc`). Aggregate + per-category tables. 3 rule-based answer-quality metrics (refusal detection with length cap, lexical-overlap groundedness, refusal-quality). 5 agent-loop sample runs (one query per category). LLM-as-judge faithfulness on 3 queries with rule-based agreement verdicts (per Zheng et al. 2023's documented biases). Total wall-clock ~3 minutes; total cost ~$0.02 at gpt-4o-mini rates.

  - `concepts/evaluation/what-is-rag-evaluation.md` — ~10 min orientation page. The retrieval/generation split as the foundational distinction; offline vs online evaluation; correctness vs groundedness (the 2x2 matrix that catches the trap most teams fall into); what evaluation can and can't tell you; the progression to the next three pages. References Es et al. 2023 (RAGAS), Min et al. 2023 (FActScore), Zheng et al. NeurIPS 2023 (LLM-as-judge canonical paper), Manning et al. (IR textbook), Thakur et al. 2021 (BEIR).
  - `concepts/evaluation/eval-set-construction.md` — ~10 min foundation page. Why 30-50 hand-curated queries beat 1,000 synthetic ones (the bias-in-volume argument); per-entry shape (`query`, `expected_doc`, `expected_chunks`, `reference_answer`, `category`, `failure_label`); target distribution table for the seed set; sourcing patterns (sit-with-corpus, mine-support-tickets, use-failure-modes-as-checklist, ask-domain-experts, walk-corpus-structurally); strict vs loose chunk annotation tradeoff; reference-answer caveats (most teams over-annotate; correctness is rarely well-defined); failure-label tagging as the diagnostic multiplier; common pitfalls (only-team-vocabulary queries, only-happy-path coverage, ignoring the per-category slice). References Manning et al., BEIR, MS MARCO, RAGAS, ARES (Saad-Falcon et al.).
  - `concepts/evaluation/retrieval-metrics.md` — ~11 min metrics page. Six metrics in detail with engineer-friendly Python formulas: hits@k (binary coverage), recall@k (completeness), precision@k (noise in top-k), MRR (rank-of-first), nDCG@k (graded-relevance with log discount; flagged as overrated for RAG vs classical IR), mean rank of expected chunk (the debugging metric). Each section: formula in Python, "what it reveals" / "what it hides" / "when it's the right metric" / gotchas. "Which metrics to compute when" decision tree (debugging vs A/B vs benchmarking vs CI). "The discipline of looking at the distribution" callout — histograms not means. References Manning et al., Järvelin & Kekäläinen 2002 (original DCG), Craswell 2009 (MRR), BEIR, MS MARCO, Anthropic's contextual retrieval (real-world example of metric choice).
  - `concepts/evaluation/answer-quality-metrics.md` — ~11 min generation-side page. Five metrics: faithfulness (every claim follows from chunks), groundedness (every claim has a citation), citation accuracy (cited chunks actually support the cited claim), answer relevance (addresses the query), refusal quality (off-corpus → refuses out-of-sample). Rule-based vs LLM-as-judge tradeoff table. Zheng et al. NeurIPS 2023's three documented biases — position, verbosity, self-enhancement — with mitigations for each. RAGAS / TruLens / DeepEval mentioned by name as future production tools (verified actively maintained 2026 via primary-source web search; atlan.com 2026 comparison and callsphere.ai 2026 framework-pattern piece cross-confirm). Cost calibration ballpark for LLM-as-judge ($0.50/run for 50-question eval). "What evaluation can't measure" (corpus correctness, user satisfaction, long-term effects). References Zheng et al. 2023, Min et al. FActScore, Es et al. RAGAS, Saad-Falcon et al. ARES, Liu et al. G-Eval, Chiang & Lee 2023.

Goal: build a from-scratch eval harness producing comparison tables across all Path 02 retrieval strategies sliced by query category. Steps 0-9. Anti-scope: no RAGAS/TruLens/DeepEval/Phoenix, no LangSmith/LangFuse/W&B, no CI integration, no drift detection, no A/B testing, no correctness-against-ground-truth as a headline metric. Common-gotchas section flags eval set drift on chunker changes, LLM-as-judge cost, the realistic q23 ("Who is the CEO of Anthropic?") off-corpus-with-mention edge case (BM25 6.58 — failure mode 8 in the wild), compound queries with `expected_doc: null`, and "aggregate metrics will lie to you" as the methodology lesson.
  - `labs/09-evaluating-agentic-rag/lab.ipynb` — 40-cell notebook (26 md / 14 code) implementing the eval harness. Step 0: setup; verify eval_set.jsonl present. Step 1: load + validate eval set; show category distribution histogram. Step 2: recreate Labs 06-08 pipelines as `(query, top_k) → list[chunk_id]` callables — `dense_baseline` (Lab 06), `hybrid_rrf` (Lab 07), `hybrid_rerank` (Lab 07), `contextual_rerank` (Lab 08, only if `context_cache.json` present). Step 3: implement `hits_at_k`, `recall_at_k`, `reciprocal_rank`, `rank_of_expected_doc` from scratch (~30 lines, no deps). Step 4: run the harness, build aggregate comparison table + per-category slice (lexical / paraphrase / referential) showing the story-the-aggregate-hides — referential mean rank moves 4.50 → 2.75 → 2.50 → 1.75 while lexical stays at 1.07. Step 5: unbiased synthesis on small-corpus caveats and reranker dominance. Step 6: rule-based answer-quality (groundedness via lexical-overlap-on-cited-chunks, refusal_quality via refusal-language-heuristic with length cap). Step 7: agent loop on 5 representative queries (q02 lexical, q10 paraphrase, q11 referential, q18 compound, q21 off-corpus) using `contextual_rerank` if available else `hybrid_rerank`. Step 8 (optional): LLM-as-judge faithfulness on 3 queries (q02, q11, q21) — demonstrates that the rule-based metric incorrectly scores refusal answers as ungrounded while LLM-as-judge correctly recognizes "refusal is faithful by default." Step 9: synthesis section closing Path 02 v1. Notebook outputs stripped; sample-output markdown cells throughout. Honest framing that the small corpus undersells contextual retrieval's headline gains.

Category distribution: 14 lexical / 6 paraphrase / 4 referential / 3 compound / 3 off-corpus.
